### The index-check ruling (orchestrator, under prompt 3 section 4)

- **The two sources.** Prompt 3 section 5.2 defaults to pending stubs "so CI is green from the first commit" (build-readiness decision 5), and phase 0's exit asks for `pnpm check` green "with the stubs counted". The PRD's D-33 "Until decided" line says: "No pending stub is created: under v1.5 the index check fails for each section 7 id with no case file (guardrails section 7; R-156), and the harness reports each such case as 'no automated check yet', never as passing."
- **The ruling.** The PRD line is the stricter one, because a stub that satisfies the index check with no test behind it may count as a loosening (guardrails section 10, "When unsure, treat the change as loosening"), and whether a stub counts as a case file is for the approver. So:
  - no pending stubs are created;
  - the index check fails for each section 7 id with no case file, and lists each as "no automated check yet";
  - a real case file whose test the pending wrapper keeps out of the green run (the field-state and evidence tests) counts as present, and is reported as "pending: no automated check yet", never as passing;
  - everything else in `pnpm check` must be green;
  - a stub generator, `tools/checks/index/generate-stubs.ts`, is written but not run.
- **Consequence.** Phase 0's exit item "`pnpm check` runs green with the stubs counted" does not hold until D-33 is decided. This is reported, not worked around.
- **Where recorded.** ADR 0003 (`docs/adr/0003-index-check-convention.md`), with how to reverse it.
- **Stricter choice logged.** Prompt 3 5.2 "Index-check convention" against the PRD's D-33 "Until decided" line; the PRD line is followed.

### Working rules for the run

- `company/` is never an input: `company/**` is excluded from every config and scan root (prompt 3 section 6). No figure is copied from `company/`, the mockups or the specs' transcriptions, and no owner document enters the repo.
- Phase 0 edits no file under `docs/guardrails.md`, `docs/product/`, the specs, `docs/ifc-input.md`, `docs/build-readiness.md`, `company/`, `prompts/` or `CLAUDE.md`, and no `.claude/settings*.json` or hook.
- npm and PyPI packages are pinned. No system software is installed.

## Phase 0: harness and scaffold (2026-09-25)

### Plan

**Goal** (prompt 3 section 10, "Phase 0"): every check exists and runs before any feature code. `CLAUDE.md` requires the first change that touches data or AI to create the harness, starting with the field-state and evidence tests.

**Requirements and functions in scope.** Phase 0 builds functions rather than stories. R-156 (the harness comes first) with its functions: F-VALUE-02 (derive function; its interfaces and field-state tests only), F-EXTRACT-04 (evidence verifier; its interfaces and evidence tests only), F-EXTRACT-03 (AI output schema validation; interfaces only if a test needs them), F-REGISTRY-01 (field registry validation), F-QUESTION-03 (sensitivity test and `affects` validation), F-REGISTRY-05 (reserved-term list and matcher), F-REGISTRY-06 (datasets and approval-record gate), F-RENDER-06 (render-test contract).

**The split.** The scaffold runs first and alone. Six builders then work in parallel, each in its own folders, with no edits to shared files (root configs, `package.json`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `eslint.config.js`, the Vitest and Playwright configs, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `tools/check.ts`, `tools/checks/{run-all,runner,lib,types}.ts`). The orchestrator's builder prompts govern where they differ from this list.

| # | Builder | Owns | Delivers |
|---|---|---|---|
| S | Scaffold | Workspace, root configs, package skeletons, `tools/check.ts`, `tools/checks/` runner files, `tools/run-render.ts`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, ADRs 0001-0003, this log | Every extension point below; one install of every phase 0 dependency |

Each with a self-test |

**Cases.** G2-1 and G2-8, proven on a test page that shows a bare digit and a count-up (builder 5). The field-state and evidence case files G1-8, G4-1, G4-15, G1-4, G1-10 and G13-1 exist behind the pending wrapper and are reported as "pending: no automated check yet" (builder 6; the wrapper comes off in phase 1 for field state and phase 2 for evidence verification). Every other section 7 id stays "no automated check yet" (ADR 0003).

**ADRs.**
- 0001 toolchain and monorepo (Accepted: default, reversible; build-readiness 3 items 1-2; D-33). Written.
- 0002 frontend, Vite and Fastify (Accepted: default, reversible; build-readiness decision 8; D-32). Written.
- 0003 index-check convention (Accepted: default, reversible; build-readiness decision 5; D-33; follows the PRD's stricter interim). Written.
- 0004 gates mechanism: builder 3.
- 0005 render test and the rule 2 reading: builder 5.
- 0006 and later, if a builder has a decision to record: 0006 domain interfaces and the pending wrapper (builder 6), 0007 lint bans and boundaries (builder 4), 0008 reserved-term matching (builder 2), 0009 repository checks (builder 1).

**Risks.**
- `pnpm check` stays red on the index check while D-33 is open (ADR 0003). A new failure inside the index check can hide behind the expected one, so its details separate "no case file" ids from other problems.
- New majors with little track record: Vitest 5, ESLint 10, Vite 8, TypeScript 6. TypeScript 7.0.2 is not usable yet: typescript-eslint 8.70.1 supports TypeScript below 6.1 (ADR 0001).
- pnpm 12's supply-chain policy refused Vite 8.3.1, released the day before; Vite 8.3.0 is pinned instead of adding an exclusion (ADR 0001).
- Playwright runs hung after the tests ended while the web server was started through pnpm; the web server now runs Vite directly from `apps/web` (ADR 0001). `pnpm test:render` fails at once, with a message, through `tools/run-render.ts` until `tests/e2e/render/` holds a spec.
- Parallel builders editing one file. Mitigated by folder ownership and the registry sub-entries (`./reserved-terms`, `./gates`, `./validation`, `./test-utils`).
- No GitLab project exists (build-readiness decision 11, D-33), so CI is written but runs nowhere; `pnpm check` is the only running mirror.

**Extension points** (the contract the builders follow):
- **Checks:** `tools/checks/<name>/check.ts`, default export `async () => CheckResult` (`tools/checks/types.ts`: `name`, `ok`, `summary`, `details`, optional `noAutomatedCheckYet` and `pending`). `tools/checks/<name>/selftest.ts`, default export returning one `CheckResult` per seeded bad input from `tools/checks/<name>/seeded/`; each must have `ok: false`. `run-all` fails a missing expected check (`index`, `reserved-terms`, `version-sync`, `registry`, `loosening`, `fixture-manifest`, `licences`, `config-exclusion`), a check without a self-test in `--self-test` mode, and any result with a non-empty `noAutomatedCheckYet`. Helpers: `tools/checks/lib.ts` (`repoRoot`, `listFiles` with `company/**` and `tools/**/seeded/**` ignored, `readText`, `pass`, `fail`). Guide: `tools/checks/README.md`.
- **Vitest:** `vitest.config.ts` projects `unit` (`packages/*/src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}`, `apps/*/src/**/*.test.ts`, `tools/**/*.test.ts`) and `guardrails` (`tests/guardrails/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}`, 60 s timeouts, `tests/guardrails/_support/` not collected). `tests/proposed/**` only in `vitest.proposed.config.ts` (`pnpm test:proposed`, non-blocking). Never `company/**`, `tests/e2e/**`, `tools/**/seeded/**`.
- **ESLint:** `eslint.config.js` spreads `tools/eslint-rules/index.js` `configs.recommended` last; rules and their `files` globs go only in that plugin. Type information is on for `*.ts`/`*.tsx`.
- **dependency-cruiser:** `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` `forbidden` list; it already fails imports into `company/`. Scan roots are fixed in `lint:deps`: `apps packages tools tests evals fixtures`.
- **Playwright:** `playwright.config.ts`, `testDir: tests/e2e`; project `render` = `tests/e2e/render/**/*.spec.ts`, project `e2e` = the rest; web server `vite build && vite preview` run directly in `apps/web` on http://127.0.0.1:4173; viewport 1440×900. A render spec that needs its own test page can use `page.setContent()` or a file under `tests/e2e/render/`.
- **Case files:** `tests/guardrails/<ID>.test.ts` (T) and `evals/guardrails/<ID>.yaml` (E), at the top level of those folders; support code in `tests/guardrails/_support/`. The pending wrapper is `tests/guardrails/_support/pending.ts` (builder 6); a case file that imports it is pending, and the index check (builder 1) detects that import and lists the id in `pending`. A pending test should skip itself at run time (Vitest's `context.skip()`) after the expected `NotImplementedError`, so Vitest shows it as skipped, not passed. Case titles start with their ids (prompt 3 section 12).
- **Placeholders:** `.gitkeep` in `tests/guardrails/`, `tests/proposed/`, `evals/guardrails/` and `fixtures/` is neither a case file nor a fixture; checks ignore it.
- **Scripts:** `pnpm check` (every step, then a summary), `pnpm checks`, `pnpm check:selftest`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm test:py`, `pnpm test:render`, `pnpm test:proposed`, `pnpm e2e`, `pnpm setup:py`.

**Changes to the plan, recorded at integration (2026-09-25).**
- **ADR numbers.** 0004 is the domain interfaces and the pending wrapper (planned as 0006); 0005 the gates mechanism (planned as 0004); 0006 the render test (planned as 0005); 0007 lint bans and boundaries; 0008 reserved-term matching; 0009 repository checks (and, after the review, the mockup-figure check); 0010 the registry values in "unapproved baseline v0", the registry-values ADR prompt 3 5.2 asks for with status "Proposed". Until the phase 0 review (finding 5) those values were a section of 0005; 0005 now records the mechanism only. No two ADRs share a number.
- **The pending cases grew from R-156's six to 13.** Derive (marker "phase 1 derive"): G1-8, G4-1, G4-2, G4-9, G4-10, G4-14, G4-15, G4-18, G9-2. Evidence verifier, check 1 only (marker "phase 1 verify-proposal"): G13-1. Evidence verifier (marker "phase 2 ..."): G1-4, G1-10, G2-2. Two notes, not product doc issues: G1-8, G4-14 and G4-15 sit in prompt 3's phase 2 list but need only `derive`, so their wrapper comes off in phase 1 (the wrapper fails them as soon as they pass); and `derive` takes a fourth parameter, `context` (ADR 0004).
- **Where the wrapper lives, and what makes a case pending.** The wrapper is `tests/guardrails/_support/pending.ts`. The domain builder first wrote it at `tests/support/pending.ts`, a path the index check does not read, so the 13 held-out files would have counted as real cases; it was moved at integration and the 13 imports rewritten (see "Guardrail review"). A T case is pending only when it imports the wrapper and its first line is the domain's marker `// @pending-until: phase <n> <feature>`; either one alone, or a malformed marker, fails the index check (ADR 0003, "Conventions the index check enforces"; ADR 0004).
- **Reserved-term module.** The list and matcher live in `packages/registry/src/reserved-terms.ts`; the `@sovitech/registry/reserved-terms` entry (`src/reserved-terms/index.ts`) re-exports it. Code outside the registry, the reserved-term check included, imports it only through that entry (dependency-cruiser `package-internals-only-through-exports`, added after the review).
- **Expected checks.** `EXPECTED_CHECKS` in `tools/checks/runner.ts` now also names `lint-bans`, `render` and, after the review, `mockup-figures`, so a missing folder fails `run-all`. `CheckResult` gained an optional `real` list, from which `run-all` and `pnpm check` print the guardrail case counts.
- **API start-up.** `apps/api/src/index.ts` calls `buildServer({ gates: assertGatesStartupSafe() })`, and since the review `buildServer` refuses any gate source that `assertGatesStartupSafe()` did not return (prompt 3 5.4; finding 23). The listen port is read by `apps/api/src/port.ts`.
- **The phase 0 review round (2026-09-25).** After the verifier and the read-only adversarial review, five fixers worked in parallel, each in its own area (index check and run guard; gates and registry; reserved terms, fixture manifest and mockup figures; render test; lint bans, Python lock, CI filter and brand), and the integrator then wired what crossed areas and re-ran every check. "Guardrail review" below records each finding.
- **The phase 0 review, round 2 (2026-09-25).** A second verifier and a second read-only adversarial review re-checked the harness after round 1; five fixers worked in parallel in their areas (index check, run guard and stub guard; gates, approvals and exception lists; render test; lint bans, scan roots and Python bans; reserved terms, company figures and fixture manifest), and the integrator wired what crossed areas: the run reconciliation in `pnpm check` and CI, the scan-roots list read by the reserved-term, figure and config-exclusion checks, nine more exception lists in the loosening snapshot (recorded again from scratch), the proposed runner's setup file, the root tsconfig, the CI jobs, and the docs. `EXPECTED_CHECKS` now also names `company-figures` and `scan-roots`.

### Guardrail review

#### Round 1 (2026-09-25)

**The review.** A verifier and a read-only adversarial reviewer (prompt 3 section 12) checked phase 0 on 2026-09-25 and reported 30 findings: 9 from the verifier and 21 from the reviewer; 3 high, 6 medium, 17 low, 3 for information and 1 expected. `git status` was compared before and after the read-only review. The findings are numbered 0 to 29 in the order of the review's findings file; comments in the code use the same numbers. Each real finding in the harness was fixed with a seeded bad input in the self-test of the check it concerns, one the earlier code let through, as `CLAUDE.md` asks ("each real finding becomes an executable case") for defects that no section 7 case covers.

| # | Source, severity | Finding | What was done | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | verifier, medium | A gate could be opened without the test-utils entry: `issueSource`, `gatesOf` and `createVerifiedSource` were exported from `packages/registry/src/gates/source.ts`, and a relative import reached them | Fixed. The issuing functions and the issued-source map are module-private. Production sources come only from `packages/registry/gates/` (`productionGateSource`, `assertGatesStartupSafe`); test-utils reaches the module through one internal function that can issue only a test override. New dependency-cruiser rule `package-internals-only-through-exports` | Seeds `tools/checks/loosening/seeded/boundary-imports/` (4 deep imports the earlier configuration let through, both review probes among them, plus test-utils imports and allowed controls), run by the lint-bans self-test and by `boundary-seeds.test.ts`; `depcruise.test.ts` counts them as the rule's proof; unit tests pin `source.ts`'s export list and who may import it |
| 1 | verifier, expected | Phase 0 exit item 1 does not hold: `pnpm check` is not green | Logged, no change: the index check fails by design while D-33 is open (ADR 0003) | The question below; ADR 0003 "How to reverse" |
| 2 | verifier, low | The pending wrapper could be gamed: a case file could throw `new NotImplementedError('derive')` itself and stay pending forever | Fixed. The error is branded: each stub declares its feature once at load, the constructor refuses callers without a private key, and only errors a stub threw are read as pending. The index check marks a case file that names the error malformed (`[pending]`) | Index seed `pending-self-thrown`; run seeds `G2-1` (self-thrown) and `G2-2` (look-alike class) in `tools/vitest/seeded/run/`; unit tests in `packages/domain` and `tools/checks/index/pending-wrapper.test.ts` |
| 3 | verifier, low | An eval file holding only `id: <ID>` counted as a real case | Fixed with finding 10 | As finding 10 |
| 4 | verifier, low | The render allowlist self-test accepted a failure for any reason | Fixed. `tools/checks/render/cases.ts` names each seed's expected reason; the real allowlist and screen list run first as the good control | 18 seeds (15 allowlists, 3 screen lists), each failing for its own reason; a unit test shows a seed failing for another reason is flagged |
| 5 | verifier, low | ADR 0005's Status field mixed "Accepted" with a "Proposed" part | Fixed. The registry values moved to ADR 0010 (Proposed); ADR 0005 records the mechanism only | Document change; ADR 0006's Status field was cut back the same way at integration |
| 6 | verifier, low | `tools/checks/mockup-figures.txt` did not exist, and the build log left out the hotel-name and mockup-figure bullets | Fixed. The list (127 entries, each tied to its spec line) and a `mockup-figures` check with a self-test; `mockup-figures` added to `EXPECTED_CHECKS`; both bullets recorded above | 8 static seeded trees against a TEST list, plus a run-time case that plants every real entry (210 plantings) and requires each to be found |
| 7 | verifier, low | The extractor's Python dependencies were pinned only at the top level | Fixed with finding 21 | As finding 21 |
| 8 | verifier, info | An untracked `.claude/scheduled_tasks.lock` predates phase 0; the adversarial review had not run | Logged. The phase commit stages explicit paths and leaves the lock out; the review has now run (this section) | None needed |
| 9 | reviewer, high | The index check judged a T case "real" from file text alone: options objects (`{ skip: true }`, `{ todo: true }`, `{ fails: true }`), describe-level skips, computed and destructured modifiers and empty bodies passed, and nothing checked that the test ran | Fixed in three layers. The index check reads case files with the TypeScript parser and marks every such form malformed (`[held out]`, `[empty]`). The guardrail run guard, a Vitest reporter, fails the run when a guardrail test is skipped, todo, `.only`-filtered or in fails mode other than by the wrapper, ran no test, did not finish, does not name its id, or (on an unfiltered run) was not collected. At integration the `guardrails` project gained `expect.requireAssertions`, so a test that asserts nothing fails | Index seeds `held-out-options`, `held-out-describe-option`, `held-out-fails-option`, `held-out-computed`, `held-out-destructured`, `empty-test`; 22 run seeds in `tools/vitest/seeded/run/` (20 bad, 2 controls); roots `held-out-only` and `not-collected`, which Vitest alone passes; root `no-assertion`, which the repository's settings fail and the earlier settings pass. All run by the index self-test and unit tests |
| 10 | reviewer, high | An E case file counted as real with nothing in it but its id; `status: pending` with no body counted as present | Fixed. An eval counts only with its full body (`fixture` under `fixtures/evals/<ID>/` that exists, `task`, non-empty `assertions`, `samples: 5`), and only as pending while no eval runner exists; anything less is `[eval]` and malformed | Index seeds `eval-id-only`, `eval-pending-id-only`, `eval-missing-fixture`, `eval-samples-not-5` (the earlier code passed all four) |
| 11 | reviewer, high | A gate could be opened by import (the same hole as finding 0, probed with the real gate definitions) | Fixed with finding 0; also, app code accepts a gate source only when the start-up check issued it (finding 23) | As finding 0 |

Such a string is copy unless it sits in a type, a property key, one side of `===`/`!==`, a `switch` case, or a list registered in `machine-keys.ts` (none registered; each entry must point to a real list and is a reserved-term exception for the approver). One hyphen or dash joins the words of a multi-word term | Seeds `bad-label-map`, `bad-ternary`, `bad-variable`, `bad-hyphenated-term`, `bad-machine-key-list` (a copy of the earlier logic let the first four through); the good tree shows every allowed position |
| 13 | reviewer, medium | On app screens a digit only had to be bound syntactically: any well-formed `data-value-id` bound anything | Fixed. `knownValueIds` is required for every check and every screen entry, read from the display objects the API serves (from phase 3) or `[]`; a hand-typed list is refused; any id outside the known set fails, with or without a number. G2-1's last test now asserts the opposite of before | Harness pages `g2-1/unknown-value-id-no-number.html`, the clean page with one id left out; screen-list seeds `without-known-ids`, `hand-typed-ids`, `no-screen` |
| 14 | reviewer, medium | A step-number marker passed anywhere on the page with text 1 to 8; pixels in canvas, img, video, embed and object were never failed | Fixed. A `url()` in CSS generated content, first read as text, is now treated as pixels |

The fast-check edge is logged: fast-check keeps one error per run, and all 13 pending cases call the stub before any assertion | Run seeds `G2-1`, `G2-2`, `G2-6` to `G2-9`; the remainder goes to phase 1 ("Next") |

**Found at integration after the review**, each fixed and proven as above: the lint-bans part of finding 17 had no owner and was fixed at integration; `requireAssertions` (finding 9) was applied at integration with its seeded root; `readPort` accepted "12.5" as 12 and "3000abc" as 3000 (configuration, not an engineering value), now refused and pinned by `apps/api/src/port.test.ts`; ADR 0006's Status field held extra text. One run of `pnpm check` at integration also failed on Vitest's 5 s default timeout in the config-exclusion test "passes on this repository" (1.3 s alone, over 5 s under the full parallel run, which now also starts child Vitest runs); that describe block now has a 60 s timeout, as the other heavy tests do. The run recorded under "Checks run" is the one after that change.

**Found while building and integrating, before the review:**
- **Near miss: pending cases that would have counted as real.** The wrapper was first written at `tests/support/pending.ts`, which the index check does not read, so the 13 held-out case files classified as real cases: the check would have reported them as working checks, which ADR 0003 forbids. Fixed at integration: the wrapper moved to `tests/guardrails/_support/pending.ts`, the imports were rewritten, and the index check requires the first-line marker too, so either half alone fails (`[pending]`). Proven by the index seeds `pending-without-marker`, `marker-without-wrapper` and `malformed-marker`.
- **Near miss: a swallowed error made a count-up page pass** (render builder). Under tsx, a scroll routine passed to `frame.evaluate` was wrapped in a `__name(...)` helper that does not exist in the page, and a `.catch(() => undefined)` swallowed the ReferenceError, so the scroll-triggered count-up page passed under tsx while failing correctly under Vitest. Fixed: all in-page code lives in the injected harness, which defines a no-op `__name`, and no error is swallowed. Covered by G2-8's existing expected result.
- **Stricter choices made, for the approver** (none costs the owner time; relaxing any of them later is a loosening):
  - Render test (ADR 0006): any running animation on a value element that shows a number fails, fade-in included; every Unicode number character counts as a digit (m², CO₂, ½, non-Latin digits); hidden and collapsed content is read; CSS generated content and list markers may never carry numbers. Since the review: value ids must be ones the screen holds; step numbers only in the registered stepper; unreadable pixels fail unless reviewed.
  - Gates and loosening (ADR 0005): an approval reference to a change-log row must also name the item; the production gate source fails closed; the test-utils override refuses to run outside Vitest; the snapshot also tracks `plausible`, `minorForTotals`, `qualifierRequired`, the `impactRank` order and each gate's closed behaviour; against the unapproved baseline any unrecorded value fails, tightenings included; policy anchors tie code constants to the guardrails text; a change in an unclear direction (the calibration threshold, the stage order) counts as a loosening. Since the review: the "Approved by" cell must be the approver's name alone or with a date; fewer than 18 gate files fail.
  - Lint (ADR 0007): the bans cover all of `apps/` and `packages/`, with the extra forms listed there; since the review, zero constants, filtered sums and deep imports into another package.
  - Index check (ADR 0003): stubs never count; versioned ids ("G4-2 v2") fail until a file-name form is agreed; since the review, a malformed file never counts, evals are pending at most until the runner exists, and a test that asserts nothing fails the run.
  - Reserved terms (ADR 0008), since the review: lower-case labels are copy; one hyphen or dash joins a multi-word term.
  - Fixture manifest (ADR 0009), since the review: 51 document extensions and 18 content signatures.
- **Readings recorded for review:** ADR 0007's four readings; the most consequential is reading 2 (guardrail case files may import `db` and the view-model server side directly; `tests/e2e/` may not).
- **Reserved terms in this phase's own text:** the new and revised docs, ADRs and READMEs were scanned with the phase's own matcher. The only hits are terms quoted to name them (ADR 0008's examples); none is in the author's voice.

#### Round 2 (2026-09-25)

**The review.** A second verifier re-checked the round 1 fixes and reported 9 new problems (numbered R0 to R8 below); a second read-only adversarial review reported 18 findings (A0 to A17): 4 high, 8 medium, 5 low and 1 for information (its recheck of round 1). `git status --porcelain` was the same before and after the read-only review. Five fixers then worked in parallel, each in its own area (index check, run guard and stub guard; gates, approvals and exception lists; render test; lint bans, scan roots and Python bans; reserved terms, company figures and fixture manifest), each reproducing its probes first and then making each one fail, with a seeded bad input that keeps it failing. The integrator wired what crossed areas (below) and re-ran every check. Each fix is a harness near miss with no section 7 case; the change-log row is owed (P-0-CHANGELOG).

| # | Severity | Finding | What was done | Proof (seeds are in the named check's self-test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A0 | high | A case written without the pending wrapper passed against the unbuilt stubs when it only asserted that the call throws (`expect(() => derive(...)).toThrow()`, `.rejects.toThrow()`), and counted as real | Fixed. The domain counts every stub error, caught or not; the stub guard (a setup file of the `guardrails` project) fails a test that reached a stub unless the wrapper held it out; the run guard fails a passing test with no stub-guard record (`[unguarded]`) or one showing a stub error (`[stub]`); the index check refuses `.toThrow()` naming no error (`[vacuous]`) | Run seeds G2-11 (`toThrow()`), G2-12 (`rejects`), G2-13 (stub reached at load and swallowed), G2-14 (a swallowing `_support` helper), each failing with the stub guard's message; root `seeded/unguarded/`; index seed `vacuous-to-throw`. Probe `adv0b/vt2` G8-4 re-run at integration: now malformed (`[vacuous]`, both forms) |
| A1 | high | The test-utils rule forbade only a direct import: `apps/api` -> `tests/proposed/<helper>` -> test-utils, and a guardrail case through the same helper, passed; `VITEST` was the override's only runtime barrier | Fixed. `gate-test-utils-only-from-proposed` is a reachability rule; new rules `proposed-tests-only-from-proposed` and `no-tests-from-apps-or-packages`; the gate source module issues and reads an override only when the `tests/proposed/` runner's setup file armed it for the running file (Vitest's worker state, no environment variable). The integrator registered the setup file in `vitest.proposed.config.ts` | 5 bad boundary seeds and 2 allowed stubs in `tools/checks/loosening/seeded/boundary-imports/` (probe `adv0b/dc`: 1 violation before, 6 now); `boundary-seeds.test.ts` rebuilds each earlier configuration and shows it let its probes through; runtime seed `gate-override-outside-proposed-runner/probe.mts` (all four attempts refused, with `VITEST=true`); `test-utils.test.ts` runs a child Vitest; a throwaway TEST case under `tests/proposed/` opened a gate at integration |
| A2 | high | Digits escaped the render test through a served value id: one id around a card tied every number in it, nothing compared a bound element with its display object, and `knownValueIds` could be a function returning literals | Fixed. Every check takes the display objects the screen was served; a number inside a value element must be made of the served strings (`display-mismatch`); nested value elements fail; app screens get display objects only from the registered API adapter, which reads the display-object route the page itself called; functions, hand-typed maps and look-alike sources are refused in screen entries | Harness pages `g2-1/container-ties-card.html` (the probe), `g2-1/bound-literal-differs.html`, `g2-1/nested-value-element.html`; 6 display-object seeds, 3 harness-page declaration seeds; screen seeds `function-display-objects.ts`, `function-known-value-ids.ts`, `look-alike-api-source.json`, `known-value-ids-field.json`; `api-display-objects.test.ts` against a TEST loopback server. Deferred: the real display-object route and type (phase 3) |
| A3 | high | The reserved-term source scan skipped copy in common forms (object keys shown through `Object.keys`, tagged templates, attributes on custom components, `data-*`, JSON copy files, SVG text, input values, templates and migrations outside `src`), and no rendered page was scanned | Fixed in two parts. **Found at integration of round 2**, each fixed:
- **The self-tests never ran in CI.** The `checks` job ran `pnpm run checks` and then `pnpm run check:selftest`; while the index check fails by design (ADR 0003), the second line was never reached. The job was split into `checks`, `checks:selftest` and `checks:run-evidence`, as they are separate steps of `pnpm check` (ADR 0001).
- **The config-exclusion check lost sight of the dependency-cruiser roots** when `lint:deps` became the scan-roots wrapper, whose script names no root. It now inspects the command the wrapper runs, with the list's roots; `wiring.test.ts` shows a list that would cruise `company/` fails it.
- **A seeded child Vitest run could write the parent's run guard report**, since it inherited `SOVITECH_RUN_GUARD_OUTPUT`; `run-seeded.ts` now drops the variable when it runs without the guard.
- **The company-figure list was optional in the loosening snapshot**, so a deleted list read as empty; now it is required, and a list that cannot be read fails.
- **The reserved-term and figure scopes did not read the scan-roots list**; they do now, so a folder the list adds for their scan is read.
- **Three zero fallbacks in the loosening check's own tallies** (outside the lint scope; no engineering value) were rewritten, as in round 1.
- **The integrator's own voice.** Output lines first written for the run reconciliation used a 2.8 reserved term; they were reworded before the recorded run. The new and revised docs, ADRs and READMEs were scanned with the registry's matcher; the only hits are terms quoted to name them.

**Change-log rows owed.** No section 7 case covers harness behaviour, and phase 0 edits no line of `docs/guardrails.md`, so the change-log row `CLAUDE.md` asks for, for the near misses of both review rounds and of building and integrating, is owed when phase 1 first edits the file. The draft, extended with round 2, is under "Proposals (not applied)".

#### Round 2 verification, final (2026-09-25)

The round 2 verifier (who wrote none of the code) reproduced each assigned probe: 23 fixed, 5 fixed in part, and 1 not fixed. The one not fixed was a regression: `tools/checks/loosening/check.test.ts` exceeded Vitest's 5 s default under the full parallel run, so the test step of `pnpm check` was red. The orchestrator fixed it by giving that describe block the 60 s timeout the guardrails project uses, and re-ran `pnpm check`: 8 of 9 steps pass, and the index check alone fails, by design. Residuals the verifier reported, logged for later rounds (none opens a gate or approves anything):
- A guardrail case can reach an unbuilt stub from a child process (`spawnSync` running `derive` in a fresh Node process) and assert only on the exit status; the stub guard and the `[vacuous]` rule do not see it. Phase 1: flag `child_process` and `worker_threads` in case files, like the test-double rule.
- A `_support` helper that swallows failures with `try { ... } finally { return; }` passes the `[support]` rule. Phase 1, with the fast-check helper and the `_support` pin.
- The runtime barrier on test-override gate sources reads Vitest's worker global, which code in the same process can forge. The static rules (dependency-cruiser `reachable`, no computed `import()`) remain the first line; phase 1 considers a module-private arming token.
- The company-figure scope does not read `evals/` or `prompts/`. Phase 2, when the first eval files and prompt edits land.
- The Python bans do not catch an alias of `float` or `eval()` on text. Phase 2, with the extractor.
- The partly fixed items (vacuous-assertion forms, late count-up and hover, the remaining lint bans, the fixture manifest's regeneration job, the exception-list schema tightening) are the "open items buildable now" and the phase items in Next.

**Status after phase 1:** the first three residuals (child process, `finally` return, the forgeable worker global) are closed in phase 1 (`[process]`, `[swallow]`, the arming token); the company-figure scope of `evals/` and `prompts/` and the Python aliases stay with phase 2.

### Definition of done

`CLAUDE.md` items 1 to 5, for phase 0 after round 2:

1. **The guardrail checks pass: does not hold.** The index check fails by design until D-33 is decided (ADR 0003): 89 ids have no case file and 13 are pending. Every other check runs and passes: the guardrail tests (G2-1, G2-8) under the run guard and the stub guard, with "real" matched against the run; registry validation with the sensitivity test and the loosening check (and its exception-list part); the reserved-term check (source and rendered pages); the version-sync, mockup-figure, company-figure and scan-roots checks; and the render test. Two of these run with little to check: the sensitivity test has no registered question, and the render test covers only the placeholder page and the harness pages, because no app screen exists yet.
2. **Evals: not running yet.** `prompts/`, the model id and the AI output schema did not change in phase 0, so no eval run is due, and the eval runner does not exist yet (phase 2). `eval-runs.ts` now fixes what a run must record before an E case counts.
3. **New behaviour has an indexed case: does not hold in full.** Holds for the render test (G2-1 and G2-8, real and passing, and the run passed both). The field-state and evidence behaviour exists only as interfaces, and its 13 cases are pending. The harness checks and every fix from both review rounds have no indexed case: they are proven by unit tests and by 392 seeded bad inputs in the self-tests, plus 39 in the Python bans' pytest cases. R-156's registry cases G6-1, G6-2 and G1-12 and the reserved-term cases G10-1 and G11-6 are not written yet.
4. **`docs/guardrails.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `prompts/sovitech-ai-system.md` agree: holds.** All three state v1.5 (the version-sync check passes). Phase 0 edited none of them.
5. **The summary names the rules touched and the cases: holds** (next section).

Not running yet: the guardrail evals and the eval runner (phase 2); the database tests (phase 1); axe and the e2e flows beyond the scaffold smoke test (phase 3); CI itself (no GitLab project).

**For the owner's review** (no approval needed to keep building, but each lets something through, or tightens a check):
- **Render allowlist, 4 entries** (`tests/e2e/render/allowlist.ts`; ADR 0006): `date-day-month-year` (dates, "D MMM YYYY" inside `<time datetime>`); `wizard-step-number` (step numbers: a number counts only inside the one registered `<ol data-render-stepper="wizard-step-number">` of 8 items, once per item, equal to its position; since round 2 each item may show only its number and the registered title of its step, the eight titles of US-INTAKE-01 AC2 (OD-4): Project, Documents, Building, Systems, Operations, Goals, Automation, Proposal; a tightening); `character-counter` ("{count} / {max}" from the named field); `max-file-size` (fixed copy "Max file size 500 MB", the example rule 2 names). G2-1 fails if the clean harness page stops using any of them.
- **Reviewed unreadable list, 1 entry** (`tests/e2e/render/allowlist.ts`; ADR 0006): `brand-logo`, an `img` whose file path matches `logo-white.svg` or its build output, marked `data-render-unreadable="brand-logo"`. Both logo files draw the letters SOVITECH CONTROL, a fingerprint and a building, as paths, with no text element and no number.
- **Lint allowlist, 4 entries** (ADR 0007): the rule 8 parser and the rule 9 formatting module for the number ban, `apps/api/src/port.ts` for the listen port, `packages/ui/src/tokens.css` for colour literals; and the engine's own scope for `no-total-outside-engine`.
- **Lint readings** (ADR 0007): broader bans than the letter (zero constants, filtered sums, loops that skip unknowns; since round 2 every running sum outside `packages/engine` is banned, layout offsets included, and the import ban covers `createRequire`, `import.meta.glob`, `eval` and `Function` across all of `apps/` and `packages/`); guardrail case files may import `db` and the view-model server side; "internals" read as any file; named colours flagged only in style values.
- **Reserved-term exceptions:** no allowance and no machine-key list registered; one non-copy entry, `**/README.md`, which keeps the round 1 behaviour for developer READMEs.
- **Reviewed test doubles and test-script flags: none.** Each future entry lets a guardrail case use a double, or the test script take a flag; each is listed here before it is relied on.
- **Scan-root exclusions, 21** (above). A new folder in the repository needs a line in `tools/checks/scan-roots/roots.json`; a new excluded folder is a loosening that waits for the approver, and a new scanned folder a tightening the baseline writer records.
- **Approvals read from git.** An approval resolves only from `main` at the merge base with the build branch; a placeholder approver name names nobody, and an "Approved by" date after the day of the run records nothing.
- **Tightenings made in round 2** (each flags more, none costs the owner time): a guardrail test that reaches an unbuilt stub fails; vacuous assertions, test doubles and swallowing support code are refused; "real" is matched against the run; bound numbers must be what the display objects serve; the render check waits, hovers, focuses, reads number words, SVG graphics and stepper titles, and scans rendered copy for reserved terms; the reserved-term source scan reads keys, tagged templates, component attributes, `data-*` values and data, template and SQL files; fixtures are regenerated, homes restricted to their types, embedded documents refused, 60 extensions and 24 signatures; company figures and SAUTER names are refused; every folder is classified; the Python extractor has bans; `.mts` and `.cts` are linted.
- **Third-party skill with no "Checked against" line:** `.claude/skills/frontend-design/SKILL.md`, reported by the version-sync check as a note.
- **Licences with weak copyleft:** MPL 4 (`@axe-core/playwright` and `axe-core` 4.13.0, `lightningcss` and `lightningcss-darwin-arm64` 1.32.0 and 1.33.0); LGPL 0. All are development or build tools.

### Proposals (not applied)

**P-0006-1. Clarification of rule 2, "Render test"** (from ADR 0006; for the approver, guardrails section 10). Revised in round 2 with its first line.

```diff
 - **Render test.** A rendered-screen test on every wizard step and dashboard fixture fails when a digit sequence appears outside an element bound to a value id. The allowlist covers dates and times, step numbers, character counters, and fixed interface copy on a reviewed list, such as "24 / 7", "3D / 2D" and "Max file size 500 MB".
+  - A number is bound only by a value id the screen was served a display object for, and only as that display object serves it: every number inside a value element is its formatted value, its lines or their declared parts, and a value element holds no other value element. A well-formed id the screen was not served binds nothing.
+  - Digits the guardrails require are bound, never allowlisted. Each number in a 2.8 status line or a rule 7 count, in digits or in words, is bound to a value id derived from stored state, such as `document:<id>.coverage` or `project:<id>.openItems.owner`.
+  - File names, revisions, tags and room or storey names are document or field values, and render bound.
+  - Digits with no stored-state meaning are not shown: upload percentages, file sizes, page numbers and totals in pagination, and numeric chart ticks (the chart's table view carries the bound values). Showing them bound to a value id is proposal 7.2.30.
```

- **The failure behind it.** Rule 2 lists the allowlist's categories but not which digits belong to none of them. Without this reading, a status line such as "Partly analysed (37 of 40 pages)" or "2 things for you to check" could be put on the allowlist as fixed copy, and its numbers would then pass the render test with no tie to stored state; pagination and file sizes (traceability 10.2 conflict 5; D-54) would be allowlisted or tied to made-up ids. Round 1 showed a literal wrapped in a made-up id passing; round 2 showed that a served id was not enough either: one id around a whole card tied the card's numbers.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up; every guardrail-required number is tied to stored state as served, and no number with no stored meaning is shown. Speed: neutral for owners (no question, gate or blocked state); some design details (file sizes, page numbers, numeric axis ticks) wait for 7.2.30.
- **Proving case.** G2-1 as indexed: `tests/e2e/pages/g2-1/*.html` fail and `tests/e2e/pages/clean.html` passes, including a rule 7 count whose number is in a value element, a served id around a card that fails, and the clean page failing once one of its display objects is left out. When the StatusLine component exists (phase 3), a case that renders a 2.8 status line with its number outside a value element and fails.
- **Classification.** Wording that clarifies the rule without changing behaviour (section 10, "What Claude Code may apply alone"). Not applied, because phase 0 edits no line of `docs/guardrails.md`; listed as prompt 3 section 7 asks.

**P-0-GITIGNORE. Ignore the owner-document formats the fixture-manifest check now knows** (from round 1 finding 15, extended in round 2 with the media and vector formats of reverify finding 3, and bearing on adversarial finding 9's `company/` point; for the owner, because prompt 3 section 12 allows `.gitignore` lines for generated output only).

`/fixtures/ifc/perf/` stays at the bottom of the file, so it stays ignored after the `!/fixtures/**` line. No line is removed.
- **The failure behind it.** `.gitignore` ignores only the 10 formats of the start of the build. An owner document in any of the other formats (a phone photo of a nameplate, a site video, a CSV meter export, an `.msg` email) could be staged by `git add` without a warning; the fixture-manifest check would then fail, but only after the file is already in the working tree of the commit. Under `company/`, which no check reads, nothing would fail at all (round 2, adversarial finding 9: `company/` already tracks 65 JPEG, 7 PNG and 1 WebP files); with these lines a new file of these formats there is ignored, while the files already tracked stay tracked. `.svg` is listed because a vector export of a drawing is a document type since round 2; the brand logos sit in an exempt home, and an icon written for the app belongs inline in a component (ADR 0007).
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up (rule 13, "The repo": one more barrier before an owner document enters the repository). Speed: none for owners. Developers must force-add a new brand asset or approved screenshot outside the two exempt homes, which the check would refuse anyway.
- **Proving case.** The fixture-manifest self-test seeds `bad-document-images`, `-office`, `-models`, `-archives`, `-mail`, `-content`, `-media` and `-media-content` already fail the check; with the lines applied, `git check-ignore` on a probe file of each family, outside the three homes and under `company/`, reports it ignored.
- **Classification.** A change to a file outside code that prompt 3 section 12 routes through the build log. It tightens; nothing in the guardrails is changed.

### Product doc issues

- **D-33: prompt 3 and the PRD disagree on pending stubs.** Prompt 3 5.2 defaults to pending stubs "so CI is green from the first commit", and phase 0's exit asks for `pnpm check` green "with the stubs counted". The PRD's D-33 "Until decided" line creates no stub and has the index check fail for each id with no case file. The stricter PRD line is followed (orchestrator's ruling; ADR 0003), so phase 0's first exit item cannot hold until D-33 is decided. The next run of prompts 1 to 3 should make the two agree.
- **D-53: the correction threshold.** The PRD's D-53 interim records the calibration threshold "but not applied"; prompt 3 5.2 applies it (as proposed). Phase 0 only records the value. Phase 7, which builds G3-6, must follow the stricter reading unless the owner decides otherwise.
- **Build-readiness 2 and the third-party skill.** "Every `SKILL.md` carries 'Checked against'" cannot hold for `frontend-design`, which is installed unchanged from anthropics/skills. Either the sentence exempts third-party skills, or the owner allows a local line to be added.

### Questions for the owner

1. **D-33 (build-readiness decision 5), with D-05 (build-readiness decision 1).** May a guardrail case that is not written yet exist as a pending stub, reported as "no automated check yet", so that `pnpm check` and CI can pass from phase 0? And who is the approver named in guardrails section 10? Whether a stub counts as a case file under section 7 is the approver's reading, so both answers are needed. Until then `pnpm check` fails on the index check at every phase, by design, with each missing case named, and no gate, registry value or exception-list entry can be approved. If both say yes, the steps are in ADR 0003, "How to reverse".
2. **New files under `company/` (rule 13 "The repo"; build-readiness decision 12; D-33).** `company/` already tracks 65 JPEG, 7 PNG and 1 WebP files, and no check reads it, so a nameplate photo added there would be committed unnoticed. The second review proposed pinning `company/`'s tracked paths and blob hashes (git's index at `3622e58`, never the content) and failing any addition. The build treats `company/` as never read, so this is not built. Which do you want: (a) apply P-0-GITIGNORE, so new image, office, model, archive and mail files are ignored by git everywhere outside the three homes (`fixtures/`, `design/reference/`, `packages/ui/src/brand/`), `company/` included; (b) allow the fixture-manifest check to read git's list of paths and hashes under `company/`, without content, and fail any new file; or (c) both?
3. **Answered by default in phase 1: [ADR 0011](adr/0011-verbatim-2-8-allowances.md) (the orchestrator's ruling, Accepted: default, reversible), for the owner to confirm or reverse; see phase 1, "Questions for the owner".** ~~Allowances for 2.8's own texts (guardrails 2.8 and section 10; prompt 3 section 13; D-05).~~ Since round 2 every reserved-term allowance is an entry of an allow list in the loosening check's exception-list snapshot, so adding one fails the loosening check until the approver approves it. Phase 1 must register allowances for the badge, status-line and action texts that 2.8 itself lists as the places a reserved term may appear (and phase 3 marks those components). Does registering, word for word, a text 2.8 names count as "adding a reserved-term exception" (prompt 3 section 13), which needs the approver? If yes, the loosening check stays red from phase 1 until D-05 is decided, and phase 1's exit ("the loosening snapshot is green") cannot hold. If no, the exception-list part should accept an allowance whose text is word for word one that 2.8 lists, and fail every other; that change is written as a proposal first.
4. **Rebuilding `tools/checks/company-figures.txt` (build-readiness decision 12; D-33).** The list was built once from `company/`, and no check reads `company/`. When `company/` is refreshed from the website repository, may a manual tool read it to rebuild the list, or should the list be rebuilt by hand?

**Phase 2:** ingestion and extraction.
- The eval runner, on the contract `eval-runs.ts` reads: `packages/ai/src/evals/runner.ts`, one results record per eval at `evals/guardrails/_results/<ID>.json` (id, `samples: 5`, `passed: 5`, prompt hash, model id, schema hash), `MODEL_ID` in `packages/ai/src/model.ts`, the schema under `packages/ai/src/schema/`. Phase 2 may move the paths with the seeds and tests; if the runner's own schema check replaces the eval body check, the results-record rule stays. (This replaces round 1's "replace `EVAL_RUNNER_EXISTS`".)
- Fixture regeneration: the CI regeneration job in the no-network container, and a double run for determinism; a seed for the Python generator runner.
- The extractor: reserved-term scanning of Python string literals if it ever writes finding text; a reviewed exception to `test_bans.py` only if it needs `int()` on a typed STEP value (a proposal, not built).
- Call `assertGatesStartupSafe()` from the demo seed; validate every locator in `verifyProposal` with a strict runtime schema and index G1-13; G11-6 with the AI output validator.

**Phase 3:** the intake wizard.
- Point `DISPLAY_OBJECT_ROUTE` (a harness placeholder, `/api/display-objects`) and `parseServedDisplayObjects` in `tests/e2e/render/api-display-objects.ts` at the API's real display-object route and the view-model display-object type (`@sovitech/view-model/browser`); every `tests/e2e/render/screens.ts` entry that shows values uses `displayObjectsFromApi()`, and e2e flows call `resetRenderCheck(page)` after an owner action. (This replaces round 1's `knownValueIds` item.)
- The app side of the render contract: the shell sets `data-render-ready` on `<body>`; Value, StatusLine and Badge show only what the display object serves, and the view-model serves `parts` for pieces rendered apart; the Stepper renders one `<ol data-render-stepper="wizard-step-number">` of eight items with their registered titles; badges, status lines, action labels, qualifier labels and generated sentences that hold a reserved term carry `data-copy-kind` and a registered allowance; evidence excerpts carry `data-document-id` and `data-content-hash` and are served in the display objects.
- Derive the render screen list from the router's route table and fail any route with no entry (adversarial finding 6(c)).
- A lint or grep rule that keeps `data-render-allow` and `data-copy-kind` inside the registered components; register the UI string catalogue with the reserved-term check.
- Phases 3 to 7: add every new screen and state to `tests/e2e/render/screens.ts`.

**Phase 4:** add `new Worker(new URL(path, import.meta.url))` and SharedWorker edges to `no-computed-import` with the viewer's first worker. The viewer's canvas needs a reviewed unreadable entry, which waits for an approved exception-list snapshot.

**Phase 5:** the engine helper that applies `unknownPolicy`, with a reviewed `no-filtered-sum` allowlist entry only if it needs one; G1-2, G1-5 and G10-1; the reserved-term scan of exports.

**When the owner or the approver decides:** a tool to write approved exception-list snapshots (D-05); the dataset approval record loader (D-47); the home of an approved SAUTER catalogue dataset, which the company-figure check would flag inside its scope (when `dataset-sauter-catalogue` opens).

**Known gaps with no phase yet** (ADR 0006 and the fixers' reports): an animation gated on a clock read inside a `requestAnimationFrame` loop that starts after every wait; an inline SVG that holds some text (its shapes are not read); synthetic focus events do not move real focus; the six number words left out; reserved-term word forms not on the list (proposal 7.2.29); what reviewed unreadable elements draw (ifc-input 6.2.15, D-46); an accumulation the lint cannot see (an unrolled sum, recursion, a per-item function passed by name).

**Brand:** renaming `logo-white.svg` or the `brand-logo` marker makes the render test fail on the placeholder, by design (ADR 0006).

## Phase 1: domain, registries, database (2026-09-25)

### Plan

**Goal** (prompt 3 section 10, "Phase 1"): the value model of guardrails section 2 as code and schema, so every later feature takes the safe path by default.

**Start of the phase.** Guardrails v1.5, the section 10 approver table empty, nothing approved. The owner decided no D row ("None yet", 2026-09-25), so prompt 3 5.2's defaults apply with ADRs, and where a default and a PRD "Until decided" line differ, the stricter one applies and is logged. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is not set (not needed in phase 1). Docker runs through Colima; the Postgres and Testcontainers ryuk images from Docker Hub are approved downloads, pinned by digest. ADR 0003 stays: no pending stubs, and the index check fails for each section 7 id with no case file. Every gate stays closed.

**Orchestrator's ruling** (the 2.8 allowance question, phase 0 "Questions for the owner", item 3): an allowance whose text is word for word a text 2.8 lists, of the kind 2.8 gives it, is 2.8 itself, not an exception beyond it; the loosening check accepts it when it can match it against 2.8 at run time, and every other allowance still waits for the approver. Written as [ADR 0011](adr/0011-verbatim-2-8-allowances.md) (Accepted: default, reversible) and listed for the owner to confirm or reverse (below).

**The split.** Four builders in parallel, each in its own folders, then this integration:

| # | Builder | Owns | Functions (phases 0 and 1 build functions, not stories) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Domain | `packages/domain/`, `tests/guardrails/_support/builders.ts`, the domain's case files | F-VALUE-02 to F-VALUE-08, F-VALUE-13 (life-safety treatment), F-EXTRACT-04 check 1 |
| 2 | Harness carry-over | `tools/eslint-rules/`, `tools/checks/{index,loosening,render}/`, `tools/vitest/`, `tests/e2e/render/`, `tests/guardrails/_support/property.ts`, `packages/registry/src/gates/`, `packages/registry/src/test-utils/` | the phase 0 open items and round 2 residuals (build log, phase 0 "Next"); ADR 0011's check |
| 3 | Registries | `packages/registry/src/{units,number-parser,production,copy,datasets,formulas}/`, `packages/engine/test-formulas/`, `fixtures/datasets/`, `tools/checks/registry/sensitivity-suite.ts`, the registry's case files | F-REGISTRY-01 to F-REGISTRY-03, F-REGISTRY-05 (allowances), F-REGISTRY-06, F-REGISTRY-08, F-VALUE-09, F-QUESTION-02 (the budget setting), F-QUESTION-03 |
| 4 | Database | `packages/db/`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example`, the database's case files | F-VALUE-01 (store side), F-AUTH-02 to F-AUTH-04 (store side), F-AUDIT-01 and F-AUDIT-04 (store side) |

**Cases** (prompt 3's phase 1 list): G1-9, G1-12, G4-1, G4-2, G4-5, G4-6, G4-7, G4-9, G4-10, G4-11, G4-18, G4-19, G6-1, G6-2, G8-4, G8-7, G8-8, G8-10, G8-11, G9-2, G10-3, G13-1, and the new G4-20, G10-8 and G13-5, each written before the code it tests, with fast-check properties for derive.

**ADRs.** 0011 (the ruling), and one per decision the builders take; the amendments of 0003 to 0008 and 0010 where phase 1 changes what they record.

**Risks.** Parallel builders touching shared harness code (the stub list in `DOMAIN_FEATURES` in particular); the index check staying red by design (ADR 0003), so a new fault inside it could hide behind the expected missing ids; new exception lists that, once recorded, can only grow with the approver.

### Built

State after integration, by function id. "Built" means built and covered by the cases and unit tests named; "safe behaviour" means the guardrails' safe behaviour while a gate, dataset or decision is missing.

| Function | Source ids | State | What works now |
|---|---|---|---|
| F-VALUE-02 derive | 2.4; rule 4 | built | `derive(field, candidates, events, context)` in `packages/domain/src/field-state.ts`: pure, deterministic and independent of input order; state precedence (conflict, known, pending, not_applicable, skipped, unknown), the active candidate, each candidate's verification and status (with refusals), provisional and stale through inputs, status-line keys, the review entry. Readings: [ADR 0016](adr/0016-derive-readings.md). Since the review: derive re-refuses on read a unit outside the registry or of another dimension (`DeriveContext.unit` is required), a qualifier the field does not register, a non-owner source on a decision, a `document` value with no entry whose check matched, an inference on unverifiable evidence above low confidence, and a calculation that read no input; events it does not apply (a system supersession of a found or entered value, a withdrawal with no removed document, a system rejection, a resolution by someone not routed or without its covered set) are listed in `FieldState.refusedEvents` with a code; a lone ambiguous reading is a fact with no active value (`FactState.ambiguous`); times are compared to the microsecond (`time.ts`) (G1-14, G1-15, G3-9, G4-25, G4-27, G8-4, G8-13, G8-14). Since round 3 of the review: an engineer's rejection of the owner's own answer on a `decision` field is refused (`engineer_overrules_owner_choice`) (round 3 also read the project type, whose registry basis is `owner_choice`, as the owner's choice; round 4 reverted that to rule 4, and it is proposal P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS); a count or reading that is not a whole number of zero or more, and a choice outside the field's `options`, is `value_shape`; a calculated or estimated value from a formula that `DeriveContext.formulaDeclared` does not declare, or with no lookup, is `unknown_formula`, and a reference value from a dataset the field does not list is `dataset_not_for_field`; a skip counts only from the owner (`skip_not_owner`) and an analysis run only from the system (`analysis_not_system`) (G1-16, G1-17, G3-12, G3-13, G7-7, G8-15, G12-7). Since round 4: a `user` value on a `decision` field counts only when its author acted as the owner (`Candidate.authorRole`, which the store sets; an engineer's own entry is `choice_author_not_owner`); the project type is compared as rule 4 says again (G3-16; ADR 0016 decisions 9 and 20). Since round 5: a document's `withdrawn` event holds from the owner or an engineer, the people 2.3's `DocumentEvent` names (round 4 had held it to the owner: a tightening, now proposal P-1-DOCUMENT-REMOVAL-OWNER-ONLY), and from the system only when its `requestEventId` names such a person's own withdrawal of the same document; its `erased` event only from the owner or from the erasure function as the system (`ERASURE_EVENT_REASON`); every other one removes nothing and is listed in `refusedEvents` as a `document` entry (`withdrawal_without_request`, `erasure_outside_function`); a rejection by an engineer, or by the owner with no value of their own, that takes a side of an open conflict its author may not resolve is refused as that person's resolution would be (`resolver_not_routed`), and the conflict stays; refused events sort by a key that tells an absent value from an empty one (G4-33; ADR 0016 decisions 8, 10 and 21) |
| F-VALUE-03 conflict test | rule 4 | built | Over the whole spread, per fact and qualifier, with decimal arithmetic on BigInt; no tolerance without a reason (so none in production); units within one fact that differ are a conflict. Since the review: an unqualified value next to qualified readings in another unit matches none of them, a conflict (G4-26); resolutions also close conflicts over a value with no qualifier (G4-29) |
| F-VALUE-04 routing and stage proposal | rule 4 | built | Routed by `confirmBy` and by any `engineer_verified` candidate; the stage proposal needs the injected order (approver setting 4, proposed) and never becomes active |
| F-VALUE-05 owner corrections | rule 4 | built (domain); the screen is phase 3 | `planOwnerCorrection` returns the new `user` candidate, the `rejected` event (never on an engineer-checked candidate), the engineer queue and the `owner_corrected_inference` event. Since the review: on an engineer or `for_quotation` field an owner's rejection holds only with the owner's own later value for the same fact; alone it leaves the value and lists it for the engineer (G3-10) |
| F-VALUE-06 owner answers, skips, not applicable | 2.1; 2.4; rule 7 | built (derive reads them; the store writes them); the question engine is phase 3 | `user_confirmed` counts only from the owner on owner or either fields; `marked_not_applicable` only from a named owner or engineer with a reason |
| F-VALUE-07 revisions and the change notice | 2.3 | built (domain); ingestion is phase 2 | Superseded only by a declared revision (`supersedes` plus a `declared_revision_of` event); `revisionNotice` gives the one notice per revision; "Source document removed" and "From a superseded revision" as status-line keys. Since the review: a later declaration on the same pair replaces the earlier one, opposite declarations at the same time cancel, and every declaration on a cycle is ignored (`documents.test.ts`; the proposed case G4-30 waits, see "Proposals (not applied)") |
| F-VALUE-08 asset identity and asset events | 2.5 | built (domain and store) | One normalised tag, one asset (`UNIQUE (project_id, normalised_tag)` in the store); untagged appearances listed as possible duplicates, never counted; merge, split and remove only from `sovitech_engineer` (refused in the domain and the store). Since the review: an appearance whose document is withdrawn or erased is no longer counted; an asset with no live appearance is not countable (G4-21, G4-28). Since round 3: an appearance carries every evidence entry the store holds for it (`AssetAppearance.evidence` is a list, read in order by `readAssetRegisterInputs`) and stays live while any entry cites a document that is not removed (G4-31) |
| F-VALUE-09 plausibility | rule 8 | safe behaviour | `checkPlausibility` is built; the production registry holds no plausible range (D-93), so it reports `no_range`; G8-10 runs with a TEST range |
| F-VALUE-13 life-safety flag | rule 11; 2.5; prompt 3 5.2 | safe behaviour, behind `dataset-asset-taxonomy` | `lifeSafetyTreatment` reads every asset as possibly life-safety until its type is `engineer_verified` and an approved taxonomy knows it |
| F-VALUE-01 append-only store | 2.4; rule 4; prompt 3 5.2 "Database" | built (store side) | Postgres 18.6 store, 10 hand-written migrations (`packages/db/migrations/0000` to `0009`): subjects, documents (written once), document and analysis events, candidates, evidence locators and excerpts (keyed by project id and content hash), extracted text, candidate, field and asset events, asset identities and appearances, guardrail events, review-item openings, the rule 10 stage 3 records (`quotation_records`, no writer yet, D-20), proposal snapshots. The app role has SELECT and column-limited INSERT only; UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE are revoked and raising triggers (`ENABLE ALWAYS`) refuse them for every role; no update or delete method exists in the data-access layer ([ADR 0012](adr/0012-postgres-kysely-migrations.md), [ADR 0014](adr/0014-append-only-store-and-guard-invariants.md)). Since the review: every candidate, field and document event names the request's own user in a role that user holds, and a system event comes only from a service account (`guard_event_actor`, SVX09); a resolution names the candidates it covered, all of its field, with a reason (SVX10 and named CHECKs); the guard check also records view definitions, column defaults, identity and generation, every privilege, functions, inheritance and schema CREATE, and the relations the definer functions trust belong to `sovitech_db_access` (`sovitech_guard.relation_owners`); errors reach the app scrubbed (`StoreError`: SQLSTATE and names only) and the database logs tersely (G4-22, G4-23, G4-24, G13-7). Since round 3: a candidate names the request's own user as its author, from a source that user may create: a `user` value from an owner member or an engineer, every other source from a service account that is a member (`guard_candidate_author`, SVX11); the system writes only the engine's supersession of a calculated or estimated value and the withdrawals that follow a removed document, and a person withdraws only their own `user` value (SVX13, SVX14); resolutions only from a person, skips only from the owner, analysis runs only from the system (named CHECKs); a `document` value commits only with an entry whose check matched (SVX01), a document only with its first analysis event (SVX12), and each alternative reading is a quantity (`candidates_alternatives_shape`); `insertCandidate(request, candidate, field)` refuses a unit outside the registry or of another dimension, on the quantity or any alternative, before any write; the guard check also records default privileges, column collations and unique indexes, and refuses a collation, type or domain in the store's schemas and a constraint or unique index added to a table the guarded functions write; the runner performs one engineer verification and one erasure on TEST rows after every migration, rolled back (`self-check.ts`) (G1-14, G2-9, G4-22, G8-4; ADR 0014). Since round 4: each candidate records the role its author acted in (`author_role`), set by the author guard from the request and never writable by the app; an erased event carries the erasure function's own reason, `document_erased` (CHECK `document_events_erased_by_erasure`; the caller's reason stays in the audit event) (G3-16; ADRs 0014, 0015). Since round 5: a document is withdrawn by the owner (a member holding `owner`) or by an engineer (a person holding `sovitech_engineer`, member or not), and by the system only as the job carrying out such a person's own withdrawal of that document, which it names (`document_events.request_event_id`; CHECKs `document_events_withdrawn_by_person_or_request` and `document_events_request_only_on_system_withdrawal`, a composite foreign key to the same project and document, and SVX15); round 4's CHECK `document_events_withdrawn_by_owner` is gone (ADR 0014 decision 4) |
| F-AUTH-02 roles | prompt 3 section 10 (app roles) | built (store side); the sign-in is phase 3 | App roles `owner`, `sovitech_engineer`, `sovitech_commercial_reviewer`, `sovitech_admin` as audited events; grants only through `sovitech.grant_app_role` and `revoke_app_role` ([ADR 0013](adr/0013-database-roles-and-row-security.md)). Since the review: nobody grants or revokes their own role (SVR03); `sovitech_engineer` and `sovitech_commercial_reviewer` are granted on the operator's login only until D-13 (SVR04); the app reads accounts and roles only for itself and the project's members (`request_accounts`, `request_account_roles`) (G10-3; `access.test.ts`) |
| F-AUTH-03 project isolation | rule 13 "Isolation" | built (store side) | `FORCE` row-level security with one project-scope policy on all 23 project tables; a request scope per transaction (`withRequest`). Since the review: members are added by an existing member or the operator's login, never by themselves (SVR03); a migration cannot open or widen a policy (G13-5, G13-6). Since round 3: only a person holding `owner` who is a member of the project, or the operator's login, adds a member (SVR01); a job's service account, the demo seed or a member without `owner` never does, and the operator's login adds a job's account to the projects it works on (ADR 0013) |
| F-AUTH-04 engineer-verification guard | rule 10 | built (store side); the review endpoint is phase 7 | `sovitech.verify_candidate` is the one writer of `engineer_verified`: refuses non-app logins, non-person accounts, users without `sovitech_engineer`, other projects, demo projects and items not opened; a trigger refuses the event from any other path ([ADR 0015](adr/0015-guarded-verification-and-erasure.md)). Since the review: the demo flag follows the creator's account: only the demo seed creates a demo project, and it cannot create one without the flag (SVR05; G10-8) |
| F-AUDIT-01 guardrail event log | section 8 | built (store side) | `guardrail_events` with section 8's types; a reason must be a code, never document text; the domain returns `evidence_not_found` and `owner_corrected_inference` events |
| F-AUDIT-04 erasure | rule 13 "Erasure"; US-DOCS-21 AC8; US-ADMIN-23 AC3 | built (store side); the job that removes files is phase 2 | `sovitech.erase_document`: an `erased` event naming the owner who asked or the system; excerpts set to "[erased]"; extracted text deleted unless another document of the project has the same bytes; candidates whose evidence is gone are withdrawn; one audit event with ids and counts only. Since round 3: nothing of an erased document is stored again: text for a content hash with no unerased document left in the project, and any evidence entry or excerpt citing an erased document, are refused (SVE11); the erasure holds a lock on the document's (project, content hash) that the text and evidence writers take shared before they look, and both sides run in READ COMMITTED transactions only (SVE12); the same bytes uploaded again take text (US-DOCS-21 AC8) (G13-8; ADR 0015) |
| F-EXTRACT-04 evidence verifier, check 1 | rule 1; rule 13 | built (check 1); checks 2 to 5 are phase 2 | Evidence citing another project's or an unknown document is rejected with an `evidence_not_found` event (G13-1); the store refuses it too (`insertCandidate`) |
| F-REGISTRY-01 field registry | 2.6; rules 3, 5, 6, 7 | built; values unapproved | 32 fields (below, "Waiting for approval"), each strict; `RegistryFieldDefinition` extends the domain's 2.6 type; the production floor `required-field-missing` and `qualifiers-invalid`. Since round 3: floors, rooms and zones declare `valueShape: non_negative_integer`; validation refuses a decision or owner-choice field that anyone but the owner confirms (`owner-choice-not-owner`) and a count without its shape (`value-shape-missing`, `value-shape-invalid`); the loosening snapshot also records each field's kind, unit, dimension, qualifiers, options, value shape and writing formulas, and every formula signature, and a widening of any fails the check ([ADR 0010](adr/0010-registry-values-unapproved-baseline.md), round 3 amendment) (G3-14, G3-15, G8-16) |
| F-REGISTRY-02 unit registry | 2.7; rule 8 | built | 48 units with dimensions and lossless factors, split dimensions where no factor exists; `checkQuantityUnit` refuses a unit it cannot map or a dimension that differs, so no quantity candidate is formed ([ADR 0017](adr/0017-unit-registry-and-number-parser.md)). Since round 3: the closed registry (symbols, dimensions, written forms, factors) and the floor-notation letters are in the loosening snapshot, a new written form, unit, factor or letter, or a merged dimension, fails the check, and every unit the registry bundle carries must be the closed registry's entry (`unit-not-in-closed-registry`) (G8-17) |
| F-REGISTRY-03 number and notation parser | rule 8 "Parsing", "Floors" | built | `parseNumber` keeps both readings of an ambiguous number; `parseQuantityText`; `parseFloorNotation` (nothing reads as zero, unknown parts unrecognised). First used in phase 2 (G8-1, G8-2, G8-3, G8-6, G8-9). Since the review: the notation parser reads only the letters of rule 8's example (S, P, Mz, E, Er); D, Et, M and any other letter stay unrecognised, the level type Unknown, the text kept, until the glossary is approved (G8-12 for the numbers) |
| F-REGISTRY-05 reserved terms, allowances | 2.8 | built | `COPY_ALLOWANCES`, five entries, each a 2.8 text accepted under ADR 0011; `BADGES` (20), `STATUS_LINES` (14) and `GENERATED_SENTENCES` (2) checked against 2.8 at test time. Since the review: the allowances apply only inside the copy registries in source, slots are typed (`{date}` a calendar date, counts digits), "Formal quotation" holds only with a named stored quotation record, and on rendered pages a marked text passes only when the screen was served it (ADR 0011, decisions 10 to 15; G3-11, G10-9) |
| F-REGISTRY-06 datasets and approval records | rule 1; 2.1; G1-12 | safe behaviour | `loadDataset` refuses malformed, TEST, undeclared, unrecorded and unresolved datasets; `datasetApprovalLookup` is false for every dataset today (D-05, D-47). Since the review: every number in the TEST datasets lies in the band 9001 to 9099, so none can pass for real data; the four changed tables are version TEST-2 (`tools/checks/registry/test-datasets.test.ts`) |
| F-REGISTRY-08 systems catalogue | dashboards 8.7; prompt 3 5.2 | built | The eight step 4 systems (`packages/registry/src/production/catalogue.ts`) |
| F-QUESTION-02 budget | rule 5 | setting only; the engine is phase 3 | Confirmation budget 7 (proposed) in the registry |
| F-QUESTION-03 sensitivity test and `affects` | rule 6 | built | 11 questions and confirmations; 6 formula signatures declared with `unknownPolicy` (G1-9: none declared reads as `refuse`); the sensitivity test on the synthetic TEST fixture project: 30 of 30 question fields change a declared output |
| F-REGISTRY-04 glossary, F-REGISTRY-07 location lookup | rule 8; D-94 | not built | `dataset-glossary` closed; city stored as typed, country as an ISO 3166-1 code in a text field, until the ISO 3166 and SIRUTA datasets are approved |

**Harness work closed in phase 1** (the phase 0 "Next" items and round 2 residuals; no section 7 case covers harness behaviour, so each is proven by seeded inputs and unit tests):
- **Lint bans** ([ADR 0007](adr/0007-lint-bans.md), phase 1 amendment): thirteen rules; new `no-decimal-from-text`, `no-json-parse`, `no-rounding-outside-formatting`, `no-zero-tally`; wider `no-zero-fallback` (zero floors, empty-list fallbacks) and `no-number-coercion` (coercing arithmetic, Number aliases); three exempt scopes as exception lists; a thirteenth boundary, `test-formulas-and-fixtures-only-from-tests` (added at integration, the registry builder's request).
- **Index check** ([ADR 0003](adr/0003-index-check-convention.md), phase 1 amendment): `[process]`, `[swallow]`; the reviewed list of support modules that may catch the stub's error, pinned by SHA-256; the fast-check helper `tests/guardrails/_support/property.ts`.
- **Gates** ([ADR 0005](adr/0005-gates-mechanism.md), phase 1 amendment): the module-private arming token replaces the forgeable worker global.
- **Render allowlist** ([ADR 0006](adr/0006-render-test.md), phase 1 amendment): unreadable entries confined to their component file; fixed copy that reads as a quantity refused.
- **Loosening** ([ADR 0011](adr/0011-verbatim-2-8-allowances.md); ADR 0005): 2.8's own texts accepted at run time; the owner-review check on "For the owner's review".

**Cases.** 57 cases turned real in phase 1 (67 real in all, with G2-1 and G2-8 from phase 0):
- the pending wrapper came off ten: G1-8, G4-1, G4-2, G4-9, G4-10, G4-14, G4-15, G4-18, G9-2, G13-1 (assertions unchanged);
- the domain added eleven: G4-5, G4-6, G4-7, G4-11, G4-19, G8-11, and, moved forward from prompt 3's phase 2 because they test only derive or asset identity, G4-3, G4-8, G4-13, G4-16, G4-17 (G1-8, G4-14 and G4-15 moved forward the same way, as phase 0 recorded);
- the registry added six: G1-9, G1-12 (a synthetic stand-in shaped like the website product list, `fixtures/datasets/TEST-website-product-list-standin.json`, never the file under `company/`), G6-1, G6-2, G8-4, G8-10;
- the database added four, against real Postgres: G4-20, G10-3, G10-8, G13-5.
- the review round added twenty, each a new section 7 id (below, "Guardrails 1.6"): twelve in memory (G1-14, G1-15, G3-9, G3-10, G4-25 to G4-29, G8-12 to G8-14), six against real Postgres (G4-21, which also runs in memory, G4-22, G4-23, G4-24, G13-6, G13-7) and two that run the render check in Chromium on new harness pages (G3-11, G10-9). Each moves a proof that the fixers first wrote as a unit test into its case file; the unit tests stay as the areas' own tests. G8-4 gained a derive assertion (the same value refused on read), and G1-4 and G1-10 (pending) the unit lookup `DeriveContext` now requires.
- round 3 of the review added fourteen, each a new section 7 id: seven in memory (G1-16, G1-17, G3-12, G3-13, G7-7, G8-15, G12-7, from the domain fixer's drafts, renumbered), one in memory and against real Postgres (G4-31), two against real Postgres (G2-9, G13-8), and four that run the loosening check or registry validation on the repository's own inputs and on their seeded bad inputs (G3-14, G8-16, G8-17 and G3-15). The existing case files gained store assertions with unchanged Expected cells: G8-4 (the store's writer refuses 1,250,000 kW for annual energy before any write), G1-14 (a document candidate on unverifiable evidence alone does not commit) and G4-22 (a default privilege granted on tables created later is refused). G10-3 and G10-8 now keep to their rows: the store's stricter readings SVR03 to SVR05 they also asserted moved to `packages/db/src/access.test.ts`.
