**The phase 1 review round (2026-09-25).** After the verifier and the read-only adversarial review (below, "Guardrail review"), three fixers worked in parallel, each in its own area: the store (`packages/db`, its migrations and ADRs 0012 to 0015, the database case files, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`); the domain (`packages/domain`, the domain's case files and builders, ADR 0016); and the registries and the harness (the reserved-term, loosening and render checks, the TEST datasets, the parser, ADRs 0010, 0011 and 0017). Each reproduced every attack assigned to it in the scratchpad before fixing it, and left an executable proof. The integrator then:
- added the five seeded imports that prove the new dependency-cruiser rule `db-testing-only-from-tests` (`tools/eslint-rules/fixtures/seeded/depcruise/`: three bad, from `apps/api`, from `packages/engine` and from a store file that is not a test; two allowed, from a guardrail case and from a store test), with their expectations; the lint-bans self-test grew from 85 to 88 seeds;
- gave the pending cases G1-4 and G1-10 the unit lookup `DeriveContext` now requires, and G8-4 the domain fixer's derive assertion;
- indexed the 20 new cases in section 7, moved each proof into `tests/guardrails/<ID>.test.ts` (the fixers' unit tests stay as their areas' tests), added two reserved-term harness pages for G3-11 and G10-9 (`tests/e2e/pages/reserved-terms/sentence-slot-with-words.html`, `stage-3-label-without-record.html`, listed in `tests/e2e/render/harness-pages.ts`), and moved the index pin to 127 ids at 1.6;
- extended the two 1.6 change-log rows (new cases; near misses);
- rewrote one zero fallback the round added in a tally of the reserved-term scan (`tools/checks/reserved-terms/scan.ts`, a count of allowance entries, not an engineering value; the section 14 grep);
- added pointers to the review amendments in ADRs 0006 and 0008;
- updated this log, including the unit count (48) and the phase status.

**The phase 1 review, round 3 (2026-09-25).** The verifier re-checked the review round's fixes (four still held only in part) and named six new findings, and the read-only adversarial reviewer ran again (below, "Guardrail review", round 3). Three fixers worked in parallel, each in its own area and each reproducing its attacks on throwaway TEST databases or with the domain code before fixing them (scratchpad `build/fix3/`): the store (`packages/db`, migrations 0003, 0006, 0008 and 0009 changed in place, the new `self-check.ts`, ADRs 0012 to 0015); the domain (`packages/domain`, the domain's builders, ADR 0016); and the registries and the loosening check (`packages/registry/src/validation/`, `tools/checks/{loosening,registry}/`, the production registry's value shapes, the re-recorded baseline, ADRs 0010 and 0017). **Not built in phase 1:** checks 2 to 5 of the verifier and the strict locator schema (phase 2); the question engine, budget ordering and screens (phase 3); the engine, formula bodies and totals (phase 5; G8-7, G8-8); registry not-applicable conditions (none among the S1 fields); the rule 11 BAC-class sentence template and qualifier labels (they wait for the approver); the calculated equipment and systems counts of step 3 (phases 4 and 5); the review endpoint and sign-in (phases 3 and 7); a store refusal of revision-declaration cycles and the verifier's refusal of non-owner decisions (phase 2, "Next"); a store refusal of documents, subjects and asset rows written by a review role that is not a member (adversarial R1e; "Next"); the store's half of an engineer's rejection of the owner's choice (derive refuses it on read); a guardrail event for refused events (waits for P-1-EVENT-REFUSED). Built since round 3 and no longer open: the unit check in `insertCandidate`, SVX01 counting only matched evidence, the candidate event types a service account may write, the role of a resolution, and tying a candidate's source to the account kind and its `created_by` to the request. Built in round 4 and no longer open: derive's refusal of an engineer's own `user` entry on an owner decision (with the store's `author_role`), and the refusal, at the store and on read, of a document withdrawal from a job's account acting alone (round 5 corrected round 4's refusal of engineers, and held the job's withdrawal to a person's withdrawal it names).

### Guardrail review

**The review.** A verifier who wrote none of the code, and a read-only adversarial reviewer (Read, Grep, Glob and Bash only; `git status` compared before and after), checked phase 1 on 2026-09-25, confirming their attacks against real Postgres and the domain code in throwaway databases: 36 findings, 14 from the verifier (V0 to V13) and 22 from the reviewer (A0 to A21), numbered as in the review's findings file; 4 high, 16 medium, 14 low and 2 for information. Three fixers then worked in parallel, each in its own area (Built, "The phase 1 review round"), reproducing each attack first and leaving an executable proof; the integrator wired what crossed areas, indexed the new cases and re-ran every check. Where a finding exposed a rule's behaviour with no indexed case, the proof is now a section 7 case (Built, "Guardrails 1.6").

| # | Source, severity | Finding | What was done | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| V0 | verifier, high | A migration could weaken the store's guards unnoticed: the guard check recorded no view definitions or column defaults, so an ordinary migration replaced the current-roles view (every account an engineer: cross-project reads, and `engineer_verified` from a person holding no engineer role) and moved a time default to 2000-01-01 | Fixed. Migration 0009 gives the relations the definer functions trust (`app_users`, `app_role_events`, `app_user_roles`, `projects`, `project_members`) to `sovitech_db_access`, recorded in `sovitech_guard.relation_owners`; `user_holds_role` reads the role events directly; the recorded shape now holds each column's default, identity and generation, each view's query and options, and every privilege at table and column level; the check also names an unregistered function in `sovitech`, a definer function outside the registry, inheritance, and CREATE on the two schemas (ADR 0013, ADR 0014) | G4-22 (14 attempts, each refused for its own reason; stored rows, the recorded shape and every account's roles unchanged; a no-role account reads and verifies nothing), G13-6; `packages/db/src/guards.test.ts`, "a migration cannot weaken a guard" (23 attempts) and the same attempts on the migrator's own login; a mutation run with the guards removed failed the target tests |
| V1 | verifier, medium | An admin wrote `engineer_verified` after granting themself the engineer role through the app | Fixed. Nobody grants or revokes their own role (SVR03); `sovitech_engineer` and `sovitech_commercial_reviewer` are granted on the operator's login only until D-13 decides (SVR04); revoking stays allowed (ADR 0013) | G10-3: an admin's self-grant and then verify, and an admin granting the engineer role to an account they created, both rejected with nothing written; `access.test.ts` (self-grant of every role, self-revoke, the operator control) |
| V2 | verifier, medium | The store took an event's claimed actor and role on trust | Fixed with A3 | As A3 |
| V3 | verifier, medium | The loosening check counted every brand-new allow list as a tightening; `index.stub-aware-support-modules` widened an existing check unnoticed | Fixed. Each exception list names its check; a deny list `checks.roster` (30 entries) records the roster; a new allow list is a tightening only when its check is new to the roster, otherwise each entry is a loosening; `tests/guardrails/_support/property.ts` is recorded as a widening that waits for the approver and is reported apart (ADR 0010) | Loosening seeds `exception-list-new-allow-list-widens-existing-check` (the 135d74d baseline) and `exception-list-check-removed-from-roster`; 7 unit tests in `tools/checks/loosening/exception-lists.test.ts` |

| V5 | verifier, medium | Equipment counts ignored deleted and erased documents | Fixed. `deriveAssetRegister` takes the project's document statuses (the store's `readAssetRegisterInputs` passes them); an appearance from a removed document no longer counts, and an asset with none left is not countable | G4-28; `assets.test.ts`, "appearances from removed documents" |

| A5 | reviewer, medium | A resolution covered whatever was written before its time: a value committed late was set aside silently, and microseconds were dropped | Fixed on both sides. The store: `field_events.covered_candidate_ids`, required on a resolution together with its reason, holding only candidates of its field and the chosen one among them (named CHECKs and SVX10). The domain: the covered set decides, never a time window, and times compare to the microsecond | G4-24 (store), G4-27 (domain); `access.test.ts`; `field-state.test.ts`, "resolution coverage" |
| A6 | reviewer, medium | A mistaken revision declaration and its correction superseded both documents: the field turned Unknown with no status line | Fixed on read: `DocumentEvent.revisionOf`; a later declaration on a pair replaces the earlier one, opposite ones at the same time cancel, and every declaration on a cycle is ignored. The store refuses no cycle (derive ignores it); logged for phase 2. The case is proposed, not indexed, because its expected result rests on this reading (P-1-REVISION-CASE) | `documents.test.ts`, "a declaration is corrected, never a cycle" |
| A7 | reviewer, medium | A value in kW, or in the unit "banana", on a kWh field became known; a misspelt qualifier formed a fact of its own | Fixed on read (`unit_dimension`; `qualifier_unregistered`, with the registered `FieldDefinition.qualifiers`). The store's half is logged with V4 | G8-4, G8-14 |

| A9 | reviewer, medium | A lone ambiguous reading was read one way | Fixed: the fact is ambiguous with no active candidate; the field is provisional, and a calculation that read it is stale. No section 7 case: no rule states an expected result for account names, which the finding rates as not document data |
| A19 | reviewer, low | An owner's rejection with no value of their own made an engineer-field value Unknown | Fixed: on an engineer or `for_quotation` field it holds only with the owner's own later value for the same fact; alone it is the owner's note: the value stays and is listed for the engineer | G3-10 |

| A21 | reviewer, low | A CHECK violation's error detail could carry the failing row's text into errors and the server log | Fixed: the layer throws only scrubbed errors (`StoreError`: the SQLSTATE and the constraint, table and column names, no cause); the database logs tersely and never logs a failing statement (migration 0000, `docker-compose.yml`, the README's `docker run`). Residual logged: an input error's ERROR line still echoes its input in the server log, and the layer sends only ids made in code and typed values (ADR 0012) | G13-7; `access.test.ts` |

**Found at integration of the review round**, each fixed:
- The fixers proposed six ids, three of them twice (G4-23, G4-24 and G3-9 each from two fixers). The cases were renumbered to the next free ids, skipping G1-13, which prompt 3 reserves for phase 2.
- G1-4 and G1-10 (pending) failed the root typecheck once `DeriveContext.unit` became required: the unit lookup was added to their contexts.
- The harness page for G10-9 first failed for its own `<title>` (a digit and a reserved term); it was retitled.
- One zero fallback in a tally of the reserved-term scan (above, section 14 greps).

**Stricter choices made in the round, for the approver** (none costs the owner time; relaxing any is a loosening): the two review roles are granted on the operator's login only (SVR04, until D-13); nobody grants, revokes or adds themself (SVR03); a person account cannot create a demo project, and the seed cannot create one without the flag (SVR05); system events come only from a service account that is a member of the project; the system's withdrawal holds only once every document a candidate cites is removed (stricter than the finding asked); on a `for_quotation` field the owner confirms, an owner's rejection with no value of their own is also read as a note to the engineer; the floor-notation parser reads only rule 8's example letters.

**Not indexed, with the reason:** the revision correction and cycles (P-1-REVISION-CASE: a reading, for the approver); a section 8 kind for refused events (P-1-EVENT-REFUSED: a change to section 8); the app's scoped account reads (no rule states an expected result); the harness fixes (reserved-term scope and slots at source, the check roster, the TEST datasets, the unit count, the notation letters), which are proven by seeds and unit tests and recorded in the near-miss row.

**Reserved terms in this round's own text:** the new and revised case files, ADR additions, change-log rows and this log were scanned with the registry's matcher; the only hits are terms quoted to name them (2.8 texts, section 7 wording, identifiers such as `engineer_verified`), none in the author's voice.

#### Round 3 (2026-09-25)

**The review.** After the review round's fixes, the verifier re-checked them (four still held only in part) and named six new findings, and the read-only adversarial reviewer ran again, confirming each attack on a throwaway TEST database or with the domain code: 25 findings, numbered as in the round 3 findings file (`reverify_partly` P0 to P3, `reverify_new` N0 to N5, `adversarial` X0 to X14); 2 high, 7 medium, 12 low and 4 for information. Three fixers worked in parallel (the store; the domain; the registries and the loosening check), each reproducing its attacks first (scratchpad `build/fix3/`: `probe-before.json` and `probe-after.json` for the store, `domain/probe-before.out.json` and `probe-after.out.json`, `probe-registry.before.json` and `.after.json`) and leaving a proof; the integrator indexed the cases, wired what crossed areas and re-ran every check. Outcome: 19 fixed, 1 fixed in part, 3 written as proposals or readings for the approver, 2 logged.

| # | Source, severity | Finding | What was done | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P0 | verifier, high (A2, the store's half) | A service account stored a system supersession of a document value and a system withdrawal with no removed document; derive ignored both, the store took them | Fixed. The system writes only `superseded` on a calculated or estimated candidate and `withdrawn` with the reason `document_erased` or `document_deleted` on a candidate every cited document of which is withdrawn or erased (SVX13); a person withdraws only their own `user` value (SVX14); the erasure function's withdrawals are unchanged (ADR 0014) | `access.test.ts`, "the system writes only what the engine and the ingestion paths need" (controls: the engine's supersession, the owner withdrawing their own answer, the deletion job's withdrawal); G4-25 on read |
| P1 | verifier, medium (A6) | The store accepts a cycle of revision declarations | Logged for phase 2; derive ignores every declaration on a cycle, and the case waits for the approver (P-1-REVISION-CASE) | `documents.test.ts` |
| P2 | verifier, medium (A7, the store's half) | `insertCandidate` stored a unit outside the registry, or of another dimension | Fixed. `insertCandidate(request, candidate, field)` runs `checkQuantityUnit` on the quantity and every alternative reading and stores nothing on a refusal (`unit_unknown`, `dimension_mismatch`, `field_takes_no_quantity`); a field of another key throws | G8-4 (store assertion added); `store.test.ts` |
| P3 | verifier, medium (A10, the store's half) | SVX01 counted any evidence entry: a document value on unverifiable evidence alone was stored | Fixed. A `document` candidate commits only with an entry whose check is `text_match`, `ocr_match` or `region_rendered`; an `ai_inference` still needs any entry (SVX01) | G1-14 (store assertion added); `store.test.ts` |
| N0 | verifier, low | An ordinary migration set default privileges unseen by the guard check | Fixed with X3 | As X3 |
| N1 | verifier, low (Speed) | An unqualified 1.2 MW next to a qualified 1,200 kW is a conflict, so the owner is asked about two equal values | Not applied: comparing readings through the registry's lossless factors shows fewer "Two values" labels and turns a conflict into a confirmation, which ADR 0016 names a loosening; proposal P-1-UNIT-FACTORS, with today's reading pinned | `field-state.test.ts`, "units of one dimension (pinned until the approver decides P-1-UNIT-FACTORS)" |
| N2 | verifier, low | A service account's `conflict_resolved` as the system was stored | Fixed: CHECK `field_events_resolution_by_person` (`owner` or `sovitech_engineer` only) | `access.test.ts` (S6) |

**Found at integration of round 3:**
- **An engineer's own `user` entry sets an owner decision (medium, not fixed).** The candidate guard (X1) accepts a `user` value from a person holding `sovitech_engineer` in their own name on any field (the store does not know a field's kind), and derive reads it as the owner's choice: an engineer's "include" on Fire Safety in scope, with no owner answer, derives as the known value (reproduced with the domain code, scratchpad `build/fix3/integ3/probe-engineer-choice.out.json`), and the resolver would give it the badge "Provided by you". Rules at stake: rule 3, "Choices belong to the owner"; rule 11, Fire Safety opt-in; 2.8. It is X1's domain half, which the domain fixer left to the integrator. Not fixed in this round: the fix (on a field that holds the owner's choice, a `user` candidate counts only with the owner's `user_confirmed` event from its own author, which 2.1 gives every owner entry on an owner field) changes the setup of several derive tests and case files, so it goes to the next round with its case ("Next").
- Two fixers proposed the same ids: renumbered (above, "Guardrails 1.6").
- The domain fixer's G3-14 (the project type) was not indexed (X14).
- ADR 0007 still named 85 lint-bans seeds (88 since the review round): corrected.
- A `pnpm check` started before the new rows existed reported the new case files as unindexed and not collected; the recorded run is the one after the rows were added.

**Stricter choices made in round 3, for the approver** (none costs the owner time; relaxing any is a loosening): members are added only by a person holding `owner` who is a member, or on the operator's login, never by a job's account, the demo seed or a member without `owner`; a job's service account writes as the system and writes code sources only in a project it is a member of, even when it holds a review role; the system's withdrawal holds only once every document the value cites is removed (the round's text also allowed a system withdrawal "on its own candidate": not built, since derive would ignore it); a person withdraws only their own `user` value; text, evidence and erasure writes run in READ COMMITTED transactions only; a constraint or unique index added to a table the guarded functions write needs an admin migration; the runner's self-check.

**Not indexed in round 3, with the reason:** the project type as the owner's choice (P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS: a reading); the owner's removal of one side of an engineer-routed conflict (pinned; P-1-OWNER-REMOVAL-ENGINEER-CONFLICT); the store's stricter readings above, the concurrency and isolation tests, the analysis and alternatives checks and the invariants (the store's own tests); the unit factors (P-1-UNIT-FACTORS).

**Reserved terms in round 3's own text:** the new case files, the ADR additions, the change-log additions and this round's log text were scanned with the registry's matcher (`findReservedTerms`); none is in the author's voice.

#### Round 4 (2026-09-25)

**The items.** Round 3's closing verification (a verifier who wrote none of the code, re-running every check and probing the store and derive on throwaway TEST data: scratchpad `build/verify3/`) named four new problems and two nits, and the integration of round 3 had left one gap open. One fixer took the domain and the store together, so the shapes agree, reproducing each item first on a throwaway TEST database and with the domain code (scratchpad `build/fix4/`: `probe-domain.before.json` and `.after.json`, `probe-db.before.json` and `.after.json`), then fixing it with an executable proof; a mutation run (`build/fix4/mutation/results.txt`) removed each new guard in turn, and its proof failed each time (5 of 5 mutants killed).

Tender 6 against as-built 5 on an engineer field went from "conflict, SOVITECH will check" to known on the tender value, with no refused event (reproduced: `probe-domain.before.json` NEWA_*, `probe-db.before.json` NEWA_*) | Fixed on both sides. The store: CHECK `document_events_withdrawn_by_owner` (a document is withdrawn only in the role `owner`, which the actor guard binds to an owner member), so SVX13 then refuses the system's follow-up; CHECK `document_events_erased_by_erasure`, and `erase_document` writes its erased event with its own reason, `document_erased`, keeping the caller's in the audit event. Derive: a `withdrawn` document event holds only from the owner, an `erased` one only from the owner or from the system with the erasure function's reason; any other is listed in `refusedEvents` as a `document` entry (`removal_not_owner`, `by_missing`) on the fields whose candidates cite the document (ADR 0016 decision 21; ADRs 0014, 0015). The rest of the path is a proposal: the job's system erasure through the erasure function still needs no record of the owner's request (P-1-SYSTEM-ERASURE-REQUEST). **Found while fixing:** the erasure function wrote the caller's reason on its erased event, so the value model could not tell the erasure path from any other system event (fixed with R4-1: the function's own `document_erased`, held by a CHECK).

**Stricter choices made in round 4, for the approver** (none costs the owner time; relaxing any is a loosening): an engineer never withdraws a document, member or not (whether an engineer may delete a document for the owner is not settled by 2.3; round 4 made the store and derive refuse it; round 5 withdrew that choice as a tightening beyond 2.3 as written, now P-1-DOCUMENT-REMOVAL-OWNER-ONLY); a person who holds `owner` in the project and also `sovitech_engineer` is recorded as acting as the owner there (new Q36, one account holding two roles); a `user` value on a decision that records no author role is refused, fewer values and never more.

**Not indexed in round 4, with the reason:** the erasure function's reason code and the author role's column rules (the store's own tests, `access.test.ts`); the project type's conflict (pinned; P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS); the job's system erasure with no owner request (P-1-SYSTEM-ERASURE-REQUEST, a proposal).

**Reserved terms in round 4's own text:** the two new case files, the ADR additions, the change-log additions, the section 7 rows and this log's round 4 text were scanned with the registry's matcher (`findReservedTerms`); the hits are identifiers and quoted rule or 2.8 text (for example `engineer_verified`, "Provided by you"), none in the author's voice.

**Stricter choices made in round 5, for the approver** (none costs the owner time; relaxing any is a loosening): the system withdraws a document only as the job carrying out the owner's or an engineer's own withdrawal, which it names (a reading of 2.3, ADR 0016 decision 21; the reason G4-32 was taken back). Listed so the approver sees it, though it follows from rule 4 as written: the owner's rejection with no value of their own no longer closes a conflict routed to the engineer (R5-4); the owner's correction with a value of their own still does, as rule 4's "A correction is a resolution" says.

**Not indexed in round 5, with the reason:** the job's document withdrawal alone (G4-32 taken back: a reading); the owner's lone rejection of a side of an engineer-routed conflict (R5-4: the index pin; drafted above); the store's request column rules and SVX15 (the store's own tests); the refused-event order (a unit test); an engineer's deletion ending a conflict routed to the owner (pinned until the approver answers "Questions for the owner", item 12).

Two local variable names in the new test block (`confirmed`, `verified`) were renamed.

### Definition of done

`CLAUDE.md` items 1 to 5, for phase 1 after round 5 of the review:

1. **The guardrail checks pass: does not hold.** The index check fails by design until D-33 is decided (ADR 0003): 71 ids have no case file (51 T, 20 E) and 3 are pending. Every other check passes: the 69 real guardrail cases under the run guard and the stub guard, with "real" matched against the run; registry validation with the production floor, the owner-choice and value-shape rules and the sensitivity test (30 of 30 on the TEST fixture); the loosening check (the registry with its new per-field properties, the formula signatures, the closed unit registry and the floor letters; gates; 30 exception lists; the 2.8 texts of ADR 0011; the owner-review list); the reserved-term check; version sync at v1.6; the render test; the mockup-figure, company-figure and scan-roots checks; and each check's self-test (458 seeded bad inputs).
2. **Evals: not running yet.** `prompts/` changed only in its version line; the model id and the AI output schema did not change, and the eval runner does not exist yet (phase 2). No eval run is due, and none could run.
3. **New behaviour has an indexed case: does not hold in full.** Holds for derive and the conflict test, routing, resolutions with their covered sets, corrections, removal (a deleted document withdraws the values and assets only it supports), an engineer's rejection that never takes a side of a conflict routed to the owner, asset identity and counts, the owner's decisions (an engineer's own entry refused), value shapes, skips and analysis runs, formula and dataset lookups (G1-8, G1-14 to G1-17, G3-9, G3-10, G3-12, G3-13, G3-16, G4-1 to G4-3, G4-5 to G4-11, G4-13 to G4-19, G4-25 to G4-29, G4-31, G4-33, G7-7, G8-11, G8-13 to G8-15, G9-2, G12-7), the registries, the parser and the loosening snapshot (G1-9, G1-12, G3-14, G3-15, G6-1, G6-2, G8-4, G8-10, G8-12, G8-16, G8-17), the store (G1-14 and G8-4 at the store, G2-9, G3-16 at the store, G4-20 to G4-24, G4-31, G10-3, G10-8, G13-1, G13-5 to G13-8) and the rendered 2.8 texts (G3-11, G10-9). Does not hold, with proofs only in unit tests, store tests or seeds: a document withdrawal by the job alone, refused at the store and on read (G4-32 taken back in round 5: a reading); the owner's rejection with no value of their own of a side of an engineer-routed conflict (R5-4; its drafted case waits for the index pin); the refused-event order; the correction of a revision declaration and cycles (proposed G4-30, a reading for the approver); refused events kept only in `FieldState.refusedEvents` (no section 8 kind; proposed); the project type compared as rule 4 says, pinned until the approver decides P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS; the owner's removal of one side of an engineer-routed conflict and the unit factors (each pinned until the approver decides its proposal); the store's stricter readings (the author role's column rules, the erasure function's reason code, the system's candidate events, the field-event roles, a job's account acting only where it is a member, who adds members, the erasure lock and READ COMMITTED under concurrent writes, the analysis event, the shape of alternatives, the invariants on default privileges, collations and constraints, the runner's self-check); the app's scoped account and role reads; the plausibility check without a TEST range; the dataset loader's refusals other than G1-12; the unit dimension splits; the notation letters (their expected result waits for the approver's glossary answer); and the harness changes (seeded inputs and unit tests). Not built, so with no case: a system erasure only on the owner's request (P-1-SYSTEM-ERASURE-REQUEST).
4. **`docs/guardrails.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `prompts/sovitech-ai-system.md` agree: holds.** All three state v1.6 (version-sync check); every review round added rows within 1.6, as the same phase.
5. **The summary names the rules touched and the cases: holds** (next section).

Not running yet: the guardrail evals and the eval runner (phase 2); axe and the e2e flows beyond the scaffold smoke test (phase 3); CI (no GitLab project, and its Vitest job would need a Docker service).

**Recorded in round 3 of the review** (2026-09-25, none approved, D-05; ADR 0010, round 3 amendment; ADR 0017):
- per field: `kind`, `unit`, `dimension`, `qualifiers` (sorted), `options` (sorted), `valueShape`, and `formulas` (the declared formulas whose outputs name the field). Units and dimensions: gross floor area `m2` (area); floors, rooms and zones `count` (count); no other field has one. Qualifiers: gross floor area `gross_total`; floors `attic`, `below_ground`, `ground`, `mezzanine`, `roof_plant`, `semi_basement`, `setback_or_technical`, `upper`; rooms `all_spaces`, `guest_rooms`, `keys`; zones `fire_compartment`, `hvac_control`, `lighting`. Options as the table above lists them (the systems in scope `exclude`, `include`; the automation areas and goals `not_selected`, `selected`). Value shape `non_negative_integer` on floors, rooms and zones. No formula writes any field;
- beside the fields: `formulas`, the six signatures (`capexIndicativeRange@1` `refuse`, estimated; `capexPreliminaryEstimate@1`, `operatingEnergyEstimate@1`, `pointsEstimate@1` and `savingsEstimate@1` `range_over_options`, estimated; `measurePriority@1` `refuse`, calculated); `datasets`, none; `units`, the closed unit registry (48 units in 30 dimensions, 62 written forms, 36 factors); `floorNotationLetters`, S below ground, P ground, Mz mezzanine, E upper, Er setback or technical.

**Questions and confirmations** (11): `q.project.name`, `q.project.type`, `q.project.location` (step 1); `q.project.systemsInScope` (step 4); `q.building.type`, `c.building.type` (the inline "Yes, it's a hotel"), `q.project.occupancy`, `q.project.operatingSchedule` (step 5); `q.project.goals` (step 6); `q.project.automationAreas` (step 7); `q.building.grossFloorArea` (step 8, inline, once, while missing). No question for area, floors, rooms or zones on step 3, no notes and no "Other" text: each would be a new question.

**Formula signatures** (declared, no bodies), each waiting for its dataset, gate and method; until then each output reads "Not available yet", naming what is missing: `capexIndicativeRange@1` (`refuse`) and `capexPreliminaryEstimate@1` (`range_over_options`): `dataset-cost-ranges`, D-92; `pointsEstimate@1` (`range_over_options`): `dataset-point-templates`, D-92; `operatingEnergyEstimate@1` and `savingsEstimate@1` (`range_over_options`): benchmarks, climate and savings factors, D-93, with costs in currency also waiting for `financial-indicators` and `units-7.2.22` (D-07, D-48, D-44); `measurePriority@1` (`refuse`): SOVITECH function set v1, D-92. Template slot `template:proposal.title`.

**Speed cost, for the approver** (rule 6; prompt 3 section 10): the sensitivity test proves each step 5 to 7 answer reaches a declared output on the synthetic fixture project, with TEST datasets and TEST formulas. In the app, every output those answers reach (points, the investment stages, operating energy, savings, measure priority) reads "Not available yet" until the SOVITECH datasets and the financial method exist, so the owner answers questions whose effect they cannot see yet. The approved questions are kept.

**Reserved-term allowances accepted as 2.8 itself under [ADR 0011](adr/0011-verbatim-2-8-allowances.md)** (Accepted: default, reversible; for the owner to confirm or reverse): badge "Verified by SOVITECH" (`badge:verified_by_sovitech`), badge "Confirmed by you" (`badge:confirmed_by_you`), status line "Formal quotation" (`status_line:formal_quotation`), generated sentences "AI inference, verified by SOVITECH on {date}" (`generated_sentence:ai_inference_engineer_verified_line`) and "AI inference, confirmed by you" (`generated_sentence:ai_inference_user_confirmed_line`). The check reads them against 2.8 at the merge base with main on every run and never records them.

**Exception lists added in phase 1** (recorded in unapproved baseline v0 on 2026-09-25; each allow entry is listed under "For the owner's review"): `lint-bans.decimal-from-text-exempt`, `lint-bans.rounding-exempt`, `lint-bans.json-parse-reviewed`, `index.stub-aware-support-modules`, and, in the review round, the deny list `checks.roster` (30 entries: the checks the exception lists belong to, recorded by the writer); 30 lists in all. An entry added from now on waits for the approver (D-05).

**A widening waiting for the approver** (review round, verifier finding V3): `tests/guardrails/_support/property.ts`, the entry of `index.stub-aware-support-modules`, lets a second support module catch the stub's error, which in 135d74d only the pending wrapper could. It widens the index check's `[support]` rule, so it is recorded in the unapproved baseline with a `widening` note and reported apart by the loosening check ("widening waiting for the approver"); D-05. Deleting that entry from `packages/registry/src/snapshots/exception-lists/unapproved-baseline-v0.json` would turn the loosening check red on it until the approver acts.

| An engineer's verification of an older owner answer on a choice field (P-1-CHOICE-ENGINEER-EVENT) | rule 3; 2.4 active candidate; rule 4 | D-05 | none | 2.4's order holds: the older answer that carries an engineer's `engineer_verified` event outranks the owner's later one, and the owner's rejection of it is refused; pinned by a unit test |
| An engineer's rejection of an owner fact (P-1-OWNER-FACT-REJECTION) | rules 3 and 4 | D-05 | none | An engineer's rejection of the owner's answer on a fact holds; on a choice it is refused (G3-12) |
| Removing an option, a qualifier or a unit from the registry (P-1-OPTION-REMOVAL) | section 10; rule 7 "never ... change an answer the owner gave" | D-05 | none | Recorded as a tightening by the baseline writer |
| The owner's revision declaration or deletion that removes one side of an engineer-routed conflict (P-1-OWNER-REMOVAL-ENGINEER-CONFLICT) | rule 4 routing; 2.3 | D-05 | none | The field becomes known on the remaining value, and no engineer entry is made; pinned by a unit test |
| The loosening-sensitive properties named in section 10 "Versioning" (P-1-SECTION-10-PROPERTIES) | section 10 | D-05 | none | The check already compares the allow lists derive reads, under "Any change that lets more values through" and "When unsure" |

**Readings for the approver** (each a default recorded in an ADR; relaxing any is a loosening):
- `confirmBy` engineer on gross floor area, floors, rooms and zones (rule 3 does not give these technical facts to the owner), so rule 5's own area example and 2.8's "Two values" floors example go to the engineer queue, and G4-11's confirmation goes to the engineer for floors (ADR 0010).
- `for_quotation` on the engineer's facts and `optional` on the owner's choices; country and city as text until ISO 3166 and SIRUTA are approved (D-94); the "Other" goal not registered; Fire Safety, Access Control and Elevators never preselected (D-64 for the last two).
- An `ai_inference` that the owner settled with a `user_confirmed` event stays provisional until an engineer verifies it (2.4's letter; ADR 0016 decision 14): the building type after "Yes, it's a hotel" stays Provisional.
- Unit dimensions split where no lossless factor joins the units; a value the plausibility check cannot compare is held as outside the range (ADR 0017).
- Database, since the review round: the demo seed account acts as the owner only on a demo project (prompt 3 section 7, the demo's owner answers); the demo flag follows the creator's account (ADR 0015 decision 4, from rule 10 "Demo data"), and no source lets an owner set it; a system event comes only from a service account that is a member of the project, or from the erasure function's own withdrawals (ADR 0014); the two review roles are granted on the operator's login only while D-13 is open (ADR 0013).
- Derive, since the review round: the system's withdrawal holds only once every document the candidate cites is removed; on a `for_quotation` field the owner confirms, the owner's rejection with no value of their own is read as a note to the engineer, as on an engineer field; a stated qualifier the field does not register is refused (ADR 0016).
- **Nearest 2.8 form, for the owner** (prompt 3 5.3): an asset whose only document is deleted carries the status-line key "Source document removed" and a "For you" entry (`RegisteredAsset.statusLines`, `review`). 2.8 names that line for a field; its use on an asset is the nearest 2.8 form, not new wording. G4-28 asserts only that the asset is not counted.
- **Reserved-term slots** (ADR 0011 decision 11): `{date}` takes "12 Oct", "25 Sep 2026" or "2026-09-25"; `{count}`, `{analysed}` and `{total}` take digits. Adding a slot name or a type accepts more text, so each waits for the approver with the phase that needs it.
- Database: holding `sovitech_admin` alone gives no access to project data; a person holding both `sovitech_engineer` and `sovitech_admin` can verify (refusing that is a tightening for the approver, new Q36); erasure only by the owner who asks or the system (new Q14); erasing one of two byte-identical uploads keeps the extracted text for the other (US-DOCS-21 AC8); the stage 3 record has no two-different-people constraint yet (new Q36) (ADRs 0013 to 0015).
- Database, since round 3 of the review: a candidate's author is the request's user, and a `user` value comes from an owner member or a person holding `sovitech_engineer` (an engineer need not be a member: P-1-R3-ENGINEER-MEMBERSHIP), every other source from a service account that is a member; members are added only by a person holding `owner` who is a member, or on the operator's login, and the operator's login adds a job's account to the projects it works on; the system's candidate events are the engine's supersession and the withdrawals that follow a removed document; a person withdraws only their own `user` value; resolutions only from a person, skips only from the owner, analysis runs only from the system; text, evidence and erasure writes in READ COMMITTED only; a constraint added to a table the guarded functions write needs an admin migration (ADRs 0013 to 0015).
- Derive, since round 3 (as amended in round 4): a field holds the owner's choice when it is a `decision` (round 3's reading of the registry basis `owner_choice`, the project type today, was reverted and is P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS); an engineer's rejection of the owner's answer there is refused, and since round 4 an engineer's own entry there is refused too (`choice_author_not_owner`); a count must be a whole number of zero or more and a choice one of the field's options (a shape, never a plausibility range); a calculated or estimated value needs a declared formula, and a caller that passes no `formulaDeclared` sees every such value refused; a reference value needs a dataset its field lists (ADR 0016 decisions 8, 9, 12 and 20).
- Since round 4: a candidate's `authorRole` is set by the store from the request; a person holding `owner` in the project and also `sovitech_engineer` acts as the owner there (new Q36); a `user` value on a decision without an author role is refused; a document's `erased` event holds only from the owner or the erasure function, whose own reason code (`document_erased`) the erased event carries (ADRs 0014 to 0016).
- Since round 5: a document's `withdrawn` event holds from the owner or an engineer, member or not, and from the system only when it names such a person's own withdrawal of the same document (a reading of 2.3, ADR 0016 decision 21); a rejection by an engineer, or by the owner with no value of their own, of a side of an open conflict that person may not resolve is refused as their resolution would be (rule 4; ADR 0016 decisions 8 and 10).
- Registry, since round 3: removing an option, a qualifier or a unit reads as a tightening (P-1-OPTION-REMOVAL asks the approver whether an option removal should wait for approval); a count declares `valueShape: non_negative_integer`; the project type is recorded with the basis `owner_choice` (ADR 0010).

### Proposals (not applied)

**P-1-CASES. Applied after the review of phase 1.** G4-21, G4-22 (widened with the review's attempts: a replaced view, a default set or dropped, an added function or view, a privilege granted, a table joined by inheritance), G8-12 and G13-6 are indexed in 1.6 with their case files (Built, "Guardrails 1.6"). Nothing of it is left to apply.

**P-1-REVISION-CASE. A section 7 case for a corrected revision declaration** (for the approver; its expected result rests on a reading of 2.3, not on its words):

```diff
 | G4-29 | T | A value with no qualifier matches no qualified reading, and the person the conflict is routed to resolves it by choosing one reading | The conflict closes |
+| G4-30 | T | The owner declares document A a revision of document B, then declares B a revision of A | Only A is superseded. B's values stay active. |
```

- **The failure behind it.** Adversarial finding A6: both declarations stayed in force, so both documents were superseded and every unchecked value of either turned Unknown, with no "From a superseded revision" line and no notice (against 2.3, "Old-only values stay visible" and "Changes are announced", and rule 4). derive now reads the later declaration on a pair as replacing the earlier one, lets opposite declarations made at the same time cancel, and ignores every declaration on a cycle (ADR 0016 decision 17).
- **Why it is not indexed.** 2.3 says a document is a revision of another "only through `supersedes`", set by the owner or an engineer; it does not say how a later declaration on the same pair reads, or what a cycle means. The expected result above follows from ADR 0016's reading, which the approver may confirm (a clarification of 2.3) or replace.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up (a mistaken declaration can be corrected, and no pair of declarations silently removes both documents' values). Speed: none (no question, gate or blocked state; the owner's correction is the declaration they already make).
- **Proving case.** `packages/domain/src/documents.test.ts`, "a declaration is corrected, never a cycle" (five tests, derive included); it moves to `tests/guardrails/G4-30.test.ts` with the row. A store trigger refusing a cycle is a phase 2 option.

**P-1-EVENT-REFUSED. A guardrail event kind for refused events** (section 8; for the approver, because section 10 lets Claude Code add cases, examples and clarifications alone, and this adds an event kind):

```diff
 - `confirmation_budget_exceeded`;
-- `skipped`.
+- `skipped`;
+- `event_refused`, with its reason.
```

- **The failure behind it.** derive ignores events that break the write-path rules (a system supersession of a found value, a withdrawal with no removed document, a resolution by someone not routed or without its covered set, an owner's rejection with no value on an engineer field; since round 3 also an engineer's rejection of the owner's choice, a skip that is not the owner's and an analysis run that is not the system's; since round 4 also a document's withdrawal or erasure that does not hold, listed as a `document` entry, and since round 5 its codes `withdrawal_without_request` and `erasure_outside_function`, and a rejection of a side of an open conflict by someone who may not resolve it, `resolver_not_routed`) and lists them in `FieldState.refusedEvents` with a code, but section 8 names no kind for them, so no enforcement log records that one fired ("The app logs every enforcement as an event"). `packages/domain/src/model.test.ts` ties `GUARDRAIL_EVENT_TYPES` to section 8, and the store's CHECK allows only its types.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up (an attempted forgery or misrouting leaves a trace for review). Speed: none.
- **Proving case.** A store test that a refused event is recorded once with its code, written with the section 8 change.

**P-1-POINTERS. The rules' "Tests:" pointers** (wording that clarifies without changing behaviour; prompt 3 section 12 allows only index and change-log rows, so it is proposed). Updated for round 5:

```diff
-- **Tests:** G1-1 to G1-12.
+- **Tests:** G1-1 to G1-12, and G1-14 to G1-17.
-- **Tests:** G2-1 to G2-8.
+- **Tests:** G2-1 to G2-9.
-- **Tests:** G3-1 to G3-8.
+- **Tests:** G3-1 to G3-16.
-- **Tests:** G4-1 to G4-19.
+- **Tests:** G4-1 to G4-29, G4-31 and G4-33.
-- **Tests:** G7-1 to G7-6.
+- **Tests:** G7-1 to G7-7.
-- **Tests:** G8-1 to G8-11.
+- **Tests:** G8-1 to G8-17.
-- **Tests:** G10-1 to G10-7.
+- **Tests:** G10-1 to G10-9.
-- **Tests:** G12-1 to G12-4.
+- **Tests:** G12-1 to G12-4, and G12-7.
-- **Tests:** G13-1 to G13-4.
+- **Tests:** G13-1 to G13-8.
```

- **The failure behind it.** Nine rules' "Enforced by" lists stop short of section 7 since 1.6. **Effect on the promises:** none; a pointer. **Proving case:** none needed; the index check reads section 7, not these lines. G1-13, G12-5 and G12-6 join their pointers when phase 2 indexes them, and G4-30 if the approver accepts P-1-REVISION-CASE.

**P-1-OWNER-REMOVAL-ENGINEER-CONFLICT. The owner's removal of one side of an engineer-routed conflict** (round 3, adversarial finding X11; for the approver: it adds an engineer queue entry):

```diff
 - **Deleting a document.** Deleting a document withdraws each candidate whose evidence comes only from that document. A candidate or asset with evidence from other active documents keeps that evidence.
   - A field left with no eligible candidate returns to unknown, and appears under "For you" as "Source document removed".
+  - When the owner's deletion of a document, or a revision the owner declares, takes one side out of a conflict routed to the engineer queue, the value it took out stays listed in the engineer queue as the owner's correction.
```

In the code: derive keeps a review entry `{ list: 'sovitech_will_check', reason: 'owner_correction' }` (the existing reason) for a field whose conflict was routed to the engineer until the owner's `declared_revision_of` or document `withdrawn` event removed one side.
- **The failure behind it.** Tender 6 AHUs and as-built 5 on an engineer field: a conflict for the engineer. The owner declares the tender a revision of the as-built (2.3 lets the owner declare revisions), or deletes the as-built document: the field becomes known on the tender value and the engineer queue loses the item, so the owner has settled a technical disagreement the engineer never saw (rule 4: "Conflicts on engineer fields go to the engineer queue"; "stops the owner from being asked to arbitrate technical questions"). Nothing checks that a declared revision is plausible, and here a design-stage document replaced an as-built one.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up (the engineer sees the disagreement the owner's action settled). Speed: unchanged for the owner (no question, no gate); one engineer item.
- **Proving case.** A new case: tender 6 and as-built 5 in conflict; the owner declares the tender a revision of the as-built, or deletes the as-built; the as-built value is listed for the engineer.
- **Its mirror, since round 5.** An engineer's own deletion (2.3) of the document behind one side of a conflict routed to the owner ends it on the other side, unseen by the owner; pinned by `field-state.test.ts`, "pinned until the approver answers the engineers-and-document-deletion question", and part of "Questions for the owner", item 12. Today's reading is pinned by `packages/domain/src/field-state.test.ts`, "pinned until the approver decides P-1-OWNER-REMOVAL-ENGINEER-CONFLICT", which flips with the change.

**P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS. The project type as the owner's choice** (round 3, adversarial finding X14; round 3 built it as a default, ADR 0016 decision 20; round 4 reverted the code to rule 4 as written, so nothing of it is applied; for the approver, because rule 3's list of choices does not name the project type and the reading shows fewer "Two values" labels):

```diff
-- **Choices belong to the owner.** These are the systems to include, goals, automation areas, and occupancy or schedule where no document states them.
+- **Choices belong to the owner.** These are the project type, the systems to include, goals, automation areas, and occupancy or schedule where no document states them.
```

In the code (`packages/domain/src/field-state.ts`):

```diff
 export function isOwnerChoice(field: FieldDefinition): boolean {
-  return field.kind === 'decision';
+  return field.kind === 'decision' || field.confirmByBasis === OWNER_CHOICE_BASIS;
 }
```

and the case the domain fixer drafted in round 3, to index with it:

```diff
+| G3-<next free> | T | An `ai_inference` or `document` candidate on the project type, whose registry entry records the owner's choice (`confirmByBasis: owner_choice`), next to the owner's step 1 answer | Refused. The owner's answer stays the value, with no conflict |
```

- **The failure behind it.** The registry records the project type (a step 1 required field) with `confirmBy` owner and the basis `owner_choice`, but rule 4 as written makes a document analysed later that disagrees with the owner's answer a conflict put to the owner ("Enum candidates conflict when their keys differ"; "a document analysed later disagrees with an answer the owner gave earlier"). So the owner can be asked "Which is right?" about their own step 1 answer, and an engineer's rejection of that answer holds as on any owner fact. Round 3 applied the registry's basis in derive without the approver; round 4's verification read that as a loosening in section 10's sense ("shows fewer labels"; "When unsure, treat the change as loosening") and it was reverted (build log, "Guardrail review", round 4, R4-2).
- **Why it is not applied.** Rule 3 names the systems to include, goals, automation areas, and occupancy or schedule as choices; it does not name the project type, which mixes a choice (renovation or BMS modernization) with a fact about the building (new or existing).
- **Effect on the two promises.** Speed: up (no "Which is right?" about the owner's own step 1 answer). Truth: mixed: no document or inference could set the project type, but a document that disagrees with it would no longer show "Two values" (the refused candidate keeps its refusal code in the field state, and no guardrail event records it until P-1-EVENT-REFUSED).
- **Proving case.** The drafted case above (its id is taken when the approver decides; none is held for it). Today's conflict is pinned by `packages/domain/src/field-state.test.ts`, "pinned until the approver decides P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS", which flips with the change.

**P-1-SYSTEM-ERASURE-REQUEST. The system erases a document only after the owner deleted it** (round 4, from R4-1; for the approver, because it adds a condition on the erasure job and touches new Q14, how an owner asks for erasure other than by deleting):

```diff
 -- packages/db/migrations/0008_guarded_writers.admin.sql, sovitech.erase_document
   ELSIF p_role = 'system' THEN
     IF NOT sovitech.request_user_acts_as(v_project, 'system') THEN
       RAISE EXCEPTION USING ERRCODE = 'SVE03', MESSAGE = 'the system erases through a service account that is a member of the project';
     END IF;
+    IF NOT EXISTS (
+      SELECT 1 FROM sovitech.document_events AS event
+      WHERE event.document_id = p_document_id AND event.type = 'withdrawn' AND event.role = 'owner'
+    ) THEN
+      RAISE EXCEPTION USING ERRCODE = 'SVE05', MESSAGE = 'the system erases a document only after the owner deleted it';
+    END IF;
 // packages/domain/src/documents.ts, removalRefusal
-  if (event.type === 'erased' && event.role === 'system' && event.reason === ERASURE_EVENT_REASON) return null;
+  if (event.type === 'erased' && event.role === 'system' && event.reason === ERASURE_EVENT_REASON && ownerDeleted(event.documentId, event.at)) return null;
```

- **The failure behind it.** Since round 4 a job's service account can no longer withdraw a document, but it can still erase one through the audited erasure function as the system, with no record that the owner asked, and derive honours that erasure (it cannot tell it from one the owner asked for). So a job alone can still take one side of an engineer-routed conflict away, and the document's text and excerpts go with it. Rule 13: "When an owner deletes a document or asks for erasure, one audited erasure job does all of the following". US-ADMIN-23 AC3 lets the erased event name "the system", which the store keeps.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up (a document leaves only on the owner's request). Speed: none for the owner; the erasure job needs the owner's deletion first (an erasure request by other means waits for new Q14).
- **Proving case.** A job's system erasure of the as-built document with no owner deletion: refused, and tender 6 against as-built 5 stays in conflict for the engineer; the control: after the owner's deletion, the job's erasure holds.
- **Round 5.** The system's document withdrawal now names the person's withdrawal it carries out (`request_event_id`, SVX15). The erasure function could take the same reference, naming the owner's withdrawal (rule 13 names the owner), in place of the lookup in the diff above.

**P-1-DOCUMENT-REMOVAL-OWNER-ONLY. Only the owner withdraws a document** (round 5; round 4 built it without the approver, and round 5 took it out; for the approver, because it restricts what an engineer may do beyond 2.3 as written, a tightening):

```diff
 -- packages/db/migrations/0002_subjects_and_documents.sql, sovitech.document_events
   CONSTRAINT document_events_withdrawn_by_person_or_request
-    CHECK (type <> 'withdrawn' OR role IN ('owner', 'sovitech_engineer') OR request_event_id IS NOT NULL),
+    CHECK (type <> 'withdrawn' OR role = 'owner' OR request_event_id IS NOT NULL),
 -- packages/db/migrations/0009_guards.admin.sql, event_actor_written (SVX15)
-          AND request.type = 'withdrawn' AND request.role IN ('owner', 'sovitech_engineer')
+          AND request.type = 'withdrawn' AND request.role = 'owner'
 // packages/domain/src/documents.ts
-const PERSON_REMOVERS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(['owner', 'sovitech_engineer']);
+const PERSON_REMOVERS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(['owner']);
```

and in guardrails 2.3, a change in meaning that needs the approver's own words:

```diff
-- **Deleting a document.** Deleting a document withdraws each candidate whose evidence comes only from that document. A candidate or asset with evidence from other active documents keeps that evidence.
+- **Deleting a document.** Only the owner deletes a document; an engineer who finds one wrong leaves a note for the owner. Deleting a document withdraws each candidate whose evidence comes only from that document. A candidate or asset with evidence from other active documents keeps that evidence.
```

- **The failure behind it.** An engineer's own deletion of a document ends a conflict routed to the owner, or removes values the owner's estimate relies on, without the owner seeing it (pinned: `field-state.test.ts`, "pinned until the approver answers the engineers-and-document-deletion question"). Round 4 built this rule by default; 2.3's `DocumentEvent` gives `withdrawn` the role `sovitech_engineer`, so round 5 took it out.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up for the owner (no document leaves without the owner's action), but an engineer can no longer take back a site survey they uploaded by mistake except through the owner. Speed: no owner question is added; an engineer's correction of a wrong upload waits for the owner.
- **Proving case.** An engineer, member or not, withdraws a document: refused at the store (23514) and on read it removes nothing; the owner's withdrawal holds; the job carrying out the owner's withdrawal holds, and one naming an engineer's is refused (SVX15). Round 4's form of this proof is the removed case file, kept at `build/fix5/removed/G4-32.test.ts` in the scratchpad.

**P-1-R3-ENGINEER-MEMBERSHIP. Engineers enter site values only in projects they are members of** (round 3, store fixer; a new gate on engineers):

```diff
   IF NEW.source = 'user' THEN
     IF NOT (sovitech.request_user_acts_as(NEW.project_id, 'owner')
-            OR sovitech.request_user_acts_as(NEW.project_id, 'sovitech_engineer')) THEN
+            OR (sovitech.request_user_acts_as(NEW.project_id, 'sovitech_engineer')
+                AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sovitech.project_members AS member
+                             WHERE member.project_id = NEW.project_id AND member.user_id = sovitech.request_user_id()))) THEN
```

(`packages/db/migrations/0009_guards.admin.sql`, `candidate_author_written`.)
- **The failure behind it.** None observed: since round 3 an engineer writes a `user` value only in their own name, never in the owner's. The round's instruction read a `user` value as needing a member of the project, and the adversarial reviewer asked for it to be checked against D-13 and phase 7. Engineers are never members in today's model: they review every project through their role (ADR 0013).
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up (only engineers assigned to a project enter site values there). Speed: owners unchanged; an engineer not yet added cannot record a site-survey value until an owner or the operator adds them (D-13 decides who assigns engineers).
- **Proving case.** An engineer holding `sovitech_engineer` who is not a member writes a `user` site value: refused, nothing stored; the control: the same engineer, once added, succeeds.

**P-1-UNIT-FACTORS. Readings of one dimension compared through the registry's lossless factors** (round 3, verifier finding N1, Speed; ADR 0016 names a unit conversion inside derive a loosening). Condensed from the domain fixer's working diff (228 lines, all 112 domain tests passing on a scratch copy):

```diff
 // packages/domain/src/model.ts, UnitDefinition (the registry's UnitEntry already carries `toBase`)
   readonly dimension: string;
+  /** The unit's size in its dimension's base unit, as decimal digits, where the registry declares a lossless factor. */
+  readonly toBase?: string;
 // packages/domain/src/conflict.ts
-export function disagreementOf(field: FieldDefinition, group: readonly Candidate[]): Disagreement | null {
+export function disagreementOf(field: FieldDefinition, group: readonly Candidate[], unit?: UnitLookup): Disagreement | null {
+  // readings in different units of one dimension are compared on their base values when every unit has a
+  // registry toBase factor: no division, the absolute tolerance scaled to the unit; otherwise 'units_differ'
+export function unitsCompare(a: string, b: string, unit: UnitLookup | undefined): boolean {
 // packages/domain/src/field-state.ts (every call)
-  disagreementOf(field, fact.members)
+  disagreementOf(field, fact.members, context.unit)
+  // an unqualified value is matched against qualified readings whose units compare (unitsCompare), not only the same unit
```

- **The failure behind it.** An unqualified 1.2 MW next to a qualified 1,200 kW cooling output on an owner field is a conflict put to the owner ("Which is right?") about two equal values; two qualified values 1.2 MW and 1,200 kW are `units_differ`. Probed (scratchpad `build/fix3/domain/p-exact/probe-exact.out.json`): with the change, the qualified pair is known; 1.3 MW against 1,200 kW stays a conflict; the unqualified 1.2 MW gives one confirmation; kcal/h against kW stays `units_differ` (no lossless factor); a 1 kW tolerance scales; 1 l/s against 3.6 m3/h is known.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Speed: up (no question about equal values; a confirmation instead of a conflict for an unqualified value that equals one reading). Truth: no value is converted or stored, and only lossless registry factors count, but fewer "Two values" labels show, so it is a loosening (section 10; ADR 0016, "How to reverse").
- **Proving case.** 1.2 MW against 1,200 kW, both qualified, on an owner field: no conflict; 1.3 MW against 1,200 kW: conflict. The pinned unit test "units of one dimension (pinned until the approver decides P-1-UNIT-FACTORS)" flips when it is applied.
- **Added in round 4 (verifier, R4-5): an approximate reading is never compared across units.** The working diff ignored `quantity.approximate`, so "cca. 1,2 MW" (rule 8: "cca.", "aprox.", "~", "circa", "peste" and "about" are stored as `approximate`) would have been compared as though it were not approximate against 1,200 kW, and a conflict would go. The proposal therefore compares across units only when neither reading is approximate:

  ```diff
   // packages/domain/src/conflict.ts
  -export function unitsCompare(a: string, b: string, unit: UnitLookup | undefined): boolean {
  +export function unitsCompare(a: Quantity, b: Quantity, unit: UnitLookup | undefined): boolean {
  +  // An approximate reading (rule 8) is compared only in its own unit, as today.
  +  if (a.approximate === true || b.approximate === true) return a.unit === b.unit;
  ```

  Its case: "cca. 1,2 MW" (approximate, qualified) against a qualified 1,200 kW on an owner field stays a conflict (`units_differ`), and an approximate unqualified "about 1.2 MW" against a qualified 1,200 kW matches no reading (a conflict), never a confirmation; the control: the same pair with neither approximate is no conflict once the proposal applies. Within one unit, today's test compares an approximate reading like any other (rule 4's conflict test does not mention approximate); that is unchanged.

**P-1-CHOICE-ENGINEER-EVENT. An engineer's verification never fixes an older owner choice** (round 3, domain fixer, found while fixing X2):

```diff
 // packages/domain/src/field-state.ts, verificationOf
-      if (event.role !== 'sovitech_engineer') refused.push({ kind: 'candidate', event, refusal: 'verification_wrong_role' });
+      if (event.role !== 'sovitech_engineer' || isOwnerChoice(field)) refused.push({ kind: 'candidate', event, refusal: 'verification_wrong_role' });
```

- **The failure behind it.** Reproduced (scratchpad `build/fix3/domain/probe-verified-choice.out.json`): the owner answers "include" for Fire Safety in scope, an engineer adds `engineer_verified` to that answer, and the owner later answers "exclude" and rejects "include". "include" stays active, and the owner's rejection is refused as `owner_overrules_engineer`, because 2.4 ranks verification first and rule 4 says the owner never overrules an engineer. Fire Safety is then in scope against the owner's latest answer (rules 3 and 11).
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: up (the owner's choice follows their latest answer and reads "Provided by you"). Speed: unchanged. It sets an engineer's action aside and changes a badge, so it is the approver's call.
- **Proving case.** An `engineer_verified` event on the owner's earlier "include" for Fire Safety, then the owner's "exclude": the owner's later answer is the value. Pinned today by `field-state.test.ts`, "pinned until the approver decides P-1-CHOICE-ENGINEER-EVENT".

**P-1-OWNER-FACT-REJECTION. An engineer's rejection of an owner fact** (round 3, from the adversarial X2 fix text; not built):

```diff
 // packages/domain/src/field-state.ts, derive pass 2
-        else if (event.role === 'sovitech_engineer' && ownerChoice && candidate.source === 'user') {
+        else if (event.role === 'sovitech_engineer' && (ownerChoice || field.confirmBy === 'owner') && candidate.source === 'user') {
```

- **The failure behind it.** Rules 3 and 4 do not settle whether an engineer may set aside the owner's own answer on a fact the owner confirms (identity, use and occupancy, whether the building has something); today it holds.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: mixed (the owner's word is kept, but an engineer can no longer set aside a wrong owner fact). Speed: unchanged.
- **Proving case.** An engineer rejects the owner's "hotel" building type; the owner's answer stays the value.
- **Since round 5,** inside an open conflict routed to the owner an engineer's rejection is refused by rule 4 as written (G4-33); this proposal is only about an owner fact outside a conflict.

**P-1-OPTION-REMOVAL. Removing an option a field lists waits for the approver** (round 3, registry fixer):

```diff
 // packages/registry/src/validation/snapshot.ts, listDifference
-    added.length > 0 || removed.length === 0 ? 'loosening' : 'tightening',
+    added.length > 0 || removed.length === 0 || property === 'options' ? 'loosening' : 'tightening',
```

- **The failure behind it.** Since round 3 derive refuses a choice outside `options` (G3-13). Removing an option owners already chose would then turn their stored answers into refused values, which touches rule 7 ("never ... change an answer the owner gave"); today the writer records such a removal as a tightening.
- **Effect on the two promises.** Truth: unchanged. Speed: no owner cost, but each option removal waits for the approver. Whether a removed qualifier should read the same way is part of the question.
- **Proving case.** A seed that removes "include" from `project.scope.hvac` and expects a loosening line.

**P-1-SECTION-10-PROPERTIES. The loosening-sensitive properties that section 10 names** (a wording clarification; prompt 3 section 12 allows only index and change-log rows in `docs/guardrails.md`, so it is proposed):

```diff
-- CI compares the loosening-sensitive registry properties with the last approved snapshot. It fails on any loosening without an approval record. The properties are estimation, tolerance, criticality, `confirmBy`, the identity list, reference datasets per field, and the confirmation budget.
+- CI compares the loosening-sensitive registry properties with the last approved snapshot. It fails on any loosening without an approval record. The properties are estimation, tolerance, criticality, `confirmBy`, the identity list, reference datasets per field, and the confirmation budget, and the allow lists the value model reads from the registry (a field's kind, unit, dimension, qualifiers, options and writing formulas; formula signatures; the unit registry; the floor-notation letters).
```

- **The failure behind it.** Round 3, adversarial finding X4: widening those allow lists left the check green. The check now compares them under section 10's "Any change that lets more values through" and "When unsure, treat the change as loosening", so the wording records what the check already does. **Effect on the promises:** none on behaviour. **Proving case:** G3-14, G8-16, G8-17 and their seeds.

Still open from phase 0: P-0006-1 (rule 2 render-test clarification), P-0-GITIGNORE, and the `CLAUDE.md` "Where things are" rows for `docs/adr/` and `docs/build-log.md` (phase 0, "Proposals (not applied)").

### Product doc issues

- **US-INTAKE-09 AC1 and AC4.** AC1 says step 6 shows "the eight goal cards"; AC4 says a goal whose decision field changes no declared output is not offered. The "Other" card has no text field (onboarding Q11; prompt 3 5.2), so it changes no output and is not registered: 7 goals. Building type "Other" stays an option: the TEST fixture shows it changes outputs (no benchmark, so "Not available yet").
- **US-REVIEW-05 AC5 and AC8** show the owner confirming floors ("Did you mean the upper floors?") and the gross floor area ("Is that the total gross floor area, including basements?"), as rule 5's and G4-11's examples do. Under prompt 3 5.2's strict default (`confirmBy` engineer unless rule 3 names the owner) area and floors are engineer fields, so rule 5's first condition fails and these confirmations go to the engineer queue. The stricter default is followed; moving `confirmBy` is a loosening for the approver ("Questions for the owner", item 3).
- **Prompt 3's phase lists.** G8-7 and G8-8 are listed in phase 1 but need the engine (phase 5); G1-8, G4-3, G4-8, G4-13, G4-14, G4-15, G4-16 and G4-17 are listed in phase 2 but need only derive or asset identity, and were built in phase 1. Prompt 3 section 10 allows the move; recorded here so the next run of the prompts can align the lists.
- **Recording who grants a role (US-ADMIN-16 Notes; PRD R-154; traceability new Q39 and near miss 42).** The product docs say recording who granted the engineer role "is not a criterion, because it would be a tightening". Prompt 3 section 10 says "granting any role is an audited event", and the store records every grant and revoke with who and when (ADR 0013). The stricter prompt 3 line is followed. US-ADMIN-16 AC1 (no page grants roles; roles come from the development roles table) agrees with the review round's rule that the two review roles are granted on the operator's login only. The next run of the prompts should make the product docs and prompt 3 agree.
- **Prompt 3's "New case ids".** The review of phase 1 added 36 section 7 ids beyond prompt 3's three: 20 in its first round (G1-14, G1-15, G3-9 to G3-11, G4-21 to G4-29, G8-12 to G8-14, G10-9, G13-6, G13-7), 14 in its third (G1-16, G1-17, G2-9, G3-12 to G3-15, G4-31, G7-7, G8-15 to G8-17, G12-7, G13-8) 1 in its fourth (G3-16) and 1 in its fifth (G4-33), skipping G1-13, G12-5 and G12-6, which prompt 3 gives to phase 2, G4-30, which a proposal holds, and G4-32, which the fourth round indexed and the fifth took back before the commit. The next run of the prompts should start phase 2's new ids after these.
- **Engineers and project membership (ADR 0013; D-13; phase 7's engineer workflows).** The product docs let engineers review every project through their role; the round 3 instruction read a `user` value as needing a member of the project. The store follows the docs (an engineer's site value needs no membership) and the stricter reading is proposal P-1-R3-ENGINEER-MEMBERSHIP. The next run of the prompts should say whether an engineer is assigned to a project before entering site values.

### Questions for the owner

Only questions that change the build, with their source and D ids. Phase 0's items 1 (D-33 with D-05), 2 (new files under `company/`) and 4 (rebuilding the company-figure list) stay open as written there.

1. **ADR 0011 (guardrails 2.8 and section 10; D-05).** The orchestrator ruled that a reserved-term allowance that is word for word a 2.8 text, of its kind, is 2.8 itself, and the loosening check accepts the five registered today without the approver. Since the review, ADR 0011 also states which 2.8 texts it reads (the two tables and the "Where they are allowed" list), and the allowances hold only in the copy registries, with typed slots and the stage 3 label bound to a stored record. Do you confirm this, or should every allowance wait for the approver (which keeps the loosening check red until D-05 is decided)?
2. **The approver (D-05), for an inference the owner settled (guardrails 2.4 and rule 3; ADR 0016 decision 14).** Should the building type stay Provisional after the owner's "Yes, it's a hotel", as 2.4 reads literally ("an `ai_inference` that is not engineer_verified")? Relaxing it is a loosening.
3. **The approver (D-05, D-53), `confirmBy` on area and floors (rules 3 and 5; prompt 3 5.2; US-REVIEW-05 AC5, AC8).** Gross floor area and floors are engineer fields under the strict default, so the owner is never asked to settle their basis or which floors a number counts; the engineer is. Should either become `either`? That is a loosening and would put rule 5's own examples back in front of the owner.
4. **CI and Docker (build-readiness decision 11; D-33).** The Vitest job needs a Docker service for the database tests. May the build look up and pin `docker:dind` from Docker Hub by digest for `.gitlab-ci.yml` (not an approved download today)? Separately: installing the Docker Compose plugin (system software, so yours to do) would let `docker-compose.yml` run as written; `packages/db/README.md` gives the `docker run` equivalent meanwhile. Optional: Node 22.22 or later would allow testcontainers 12.1.0 (12.0.1 is pinned).
5. **Sign-in and role grants (D-13; build-readiness 3 item 10; new Q39).** Which sign-in replaces the development login, and, once it is decided, may a person holding `sovitech_admin` grant `sovitech_engineer` and `sovitech_commercial_reviewer` from the app? Until then the store grants those two roles on the operator's login only, nobody grants, revokes or adds themself, and a caller holding the app role's password can name any user (ADR 0013, trust boundary).
6. **Floor-notation letters (rule 8, "Floors" and "Abbreviations"; D-92, D-37, D-18).** Are the letters of a regim de înălțime beyond rule 8's example "3S+P+Mz+12E+Er" (D for demisol, Et for etaj tehnic, M for mansardă) glossary items, to be read only once the Romanian glossary is approved, or does rule 8's floor structure let the parser read them now? Until you decide, they stay unrecognised and their level type reads Unknown. Phase 2 uses the parser for G8-9.
7. **The approver (D-05), a lone ambiguous reading (rule 8; rule 5; ADR 0016 decision 4).** A single candidate read two ways ("1.500 kW" as 1.5 or 1500) now has no value and is provisional, and calculations work over both readings. Should it instead be a conflict ("Two values"), which would put a question to the owner on owner fields?
8. **The approver (D-05), two proposals from the review round.** P-1-REVISION-CASE (how a corrected revision declaration and a cycle read, 2.3) and P-1-EVENT-REFUSED (a section 8 event kind for refused events), under "Proposals (not applied)".
9. **The approver (D-05), the project type (rule 3; step 1; P-1-OWNER-CHOICE-BASIS).** Is the project type the owner's choice, so that no document or inference stands on it and a document that disagrees raises no "Two values", or a fact, so that such a document puts the owner's step 1 answer into a conflict routed to the owner (rule 4 as written)? Round 4 reverted the build to rule 4 as written until you decide; the proposal carries the diff.
10. **Engineers on a project (D-13; P-1-R3-ENGINEER-MEMBERSHIP).** Should an engineer be a member of a project before entering site-survey values there? Today the store lets any person holding `sovitech_engineer` enter a site value in their own name on any project; requiring membership is a new gate on engineers until someone adds them.
11. **The approver (D-05), round 3 proposals.** P-1-UNIT-FACTORS (equal readings in different units of one dimension are no conflict; Speed up, fewer "Two values"), P-1-CHOICE-ENGINEER-EVENT (an engineer's verification never fixes an older owner choice), P-1-OWNER-FACT-REJECTION (an engineer's rejection of an owner fact), P-1-OPTION-REMOVAL (removing an option owners chose waits for approval), P-1-OWNER-REMOVAL-ENGINEER-CONFLICT (the owner's removal of one side of an engineer-routed conflict stays listed for the engineer) and P-1-SECTION-10-PROPERTIES (wording), under "Proposals (not applied)".
12. **The approver (D-05), round 4.** P-1-SYSTEM-ERASURE-REQUEST (the job erases a document only after the owner deleted it; with new Q14), and the reading of prompt 3 section 7 that the runner's self-check, which writes `engineer_verified` through the guarded function in a transaction always rolled back, does not "write it" ("Waiting for approval"). Also, engineers and document deletion: 2.3's `DocumentEvent` gives engineers `withdrawn`, so since round 5 an engineer's own deletion holds at the store and on read, member or not, and it can end a conflict routed to the owner unseen by the owner (pinned). Should an engineer delete a document only with the owner? P-1-DOCUMENT-REMOVAL-OWNER-ONLY is the owner-only form.
13. **The approver (D-05), round 5.** P-1-DOCUMENT-REMOVAL-OWNER-ONLY (above), and the reading that the system withdraws a document only as the job carrying out the owner's or an engineer's own withdrawal, which it names (ADR 0016 decision 21; the reason G4-32 was taken back). Also the drafted case G4-34 (R5-4), which the integrator can index with the pin.

- **Done in phase 2 (part A; G4-35):** NP-A (medium): round 5's hold-back of rejections is not time-aware. A rejection that held when it was made is un-applied later when a disagreeing value arrives: the rejected value becomes eligible again and the field goes into a new conflict (verify5 probes S2, S3 and the G4-33 timing probe, on building.type). The field does not silently take a winner, but a person may be asked again about a value they rejected (rule 5 "Never ask twice"; rule 4). Fix: judge each rejection against the conflict state at the time it was made (the candidates that existed, and the routing then), and pin it with a property over arrival orders. Until fixed, the behaviour is logged here and not indexed.
- **Pinned in phase 2, proposal P-2-NP-B:** NP-B (low, for the approver): after the owner resolves an owner-routed conflict by choosing one value, an engineer's later rejection of that chosen value holds (no conflict is open), and the field becomes known on the value the owner set aside, with no refused event and no "For you" entry. It belongs with P-1-OWNER-FACT-REJECTION.
